# UI scope and feature roadmap

The proposal contains 21 platform modules across four phases. The design establishes navigation for all of them, interactive detail for the patient journey, and — as of this increment — end-to-end interaction design for the flows that carry the most clinical, privacy and operational risk. A feature being listed does not mean its full operational workflow is implemented.

## Delivered as interactive design

These flows exist on all three platforms, with the same steps, the same wording and the same refusals. They hold no real data and reach no service.

| Nurse field safety: visit timers, panic and the desk queue                    | A timer starts only when the visit code matches and is due at the booked service's own duration from `services.json` plus a grace; the nurse says she is safe (which never moves the deadline), extends in offered steps with a reason the desk can read up to a ceiling, or checks out, and signing the visit checks her out. Past the deadline the strip says so in words and the desk's queue gains the timer. Panic asks once, says the desk can see her position until a stated time and nobody else can, and says pressing it calls nobody — with the police and ambulance numbers read from `sos.json`. A second press by the same nurse for the same visit while the window is open is the same panic, never refused and never folded into another nurse's. The window always ends and does not stretch; the position goes when sharing stops, at the window or the resolution, and nothing keeps where she was. The desk sees a nurse and a suburb — never the service, the patient or the address — picks a panic or an overdue up before closing it, and cannot resolve a panic without an outcome or close an overdue with a reason that is not true of the timer. The grace and the panic window are the founder's decision of 15 September 2026 and the extension steps and ceiling are proposals with `decidedBy: null`; all four are defaults an admin changes on the back office, and the build refuses a decision that does not say who took it and when, and a proposal that loses its question. `POST /v1/safety/panics@1` is built on the engine runtime, and since 15 September so is the rest: the timer is started by `appointment.in_progress@2` and timed by its service, closed by `appointment.completed@2`, and the nurse's routes, the desk's routes and `panic.resolved@1` are the row below. Positions are simulated and the nurse-position door stays shut                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Nurse strip and desk queue on all three; desk in the Control Tower's Incidents section                                                                                                   |
| Field safety settings on the back office                                      | **Built on the engine runtime and on the web back office, 15 September 2026.** The founder decided the grace and the panic window ("panic 30, grace 60") and that Operations sets them on the admin. `packages/catalog/field-safety.json` holds each default once, beside who decided it — the founder for the grace and the window, a proposal nobody has decided for the extension steps and ceiling — and `settings` holds, for each of the four, the lowest and highest an admin may set (themselves proposals, each with its reason), the unit, the admin role that may change it, and a changelog of the defaults that the build replays. The settings routes were built at version one in `packages/engines/src/safety`, with the history in Safety's own store; version one is withdrawn in favour of `GET /v1/safety/settings@2` and `POST /v1/safety/setting-changes@2` in the shared settings shape below. A change publishes no event: no engine acts on one — Core does not read Safety's timings, because Safety sends the deadline on what it raises — and the event contract refuses an event nobody subscribes to, so the route says so in `emitsNoneBecause`. The refusals are the feature: only an admin changes a setting; a change says why and is recorded with who, when, from, to and why, and the history is only ever appended to; a value outside the range is refused, and nought or less is refused before the range is asked; steps out of order, or a step the ceiling would never allow, are refused; a change against a version no longer in force is refused; and a change applies only to visits and panics that start after it — a timer copies the settings it starts under and a panic its window, so a running visit's deadline never moves and an open window is never cut short or stretched. A phone that read the window before a change is given the window in force rather than refused, because a panic is never refused over bookkeeping. The panel shows what is in force, the default, who decided it and the range; reviews a change before it is confirmed; renders every refusal in the route's sentence; and says it is held in the tab's memory, so a reload puts the defaults back. Neither native app has an admin surface, so both use the generated defaults and say so. Care's offer expiry is Care's own setting, drawn beside these on the Configuration tab. Since the settings foundation below, the four timings are settings in the shared shape, beside who may change them and which window a stale panic opens, and the panel is the Configuration tab's Field safety group; the routes are at version two in the shared shape, and version one of both is withdrawn                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Engine and web back office (Configuration tab, linked from Operations); iOS and Android use the generated defaults                                                                       |
| Patient SOS on the engine, and next of kin                                    | **Built on the engine runtime, the web, iOS and Android, 15 September 2026 (Wave 4).** `POST /v1/safety/sos@2` records a press thinly — how it was pressed, the door `sos.json` routes the answers to, whether a condition was ticked as a yes or a no, and the area chosen from the list for the `sos-area-window` it was pressed under — and publishes `sos.raised@2`. Core opens one concern for it, owned by the desk with a nurse behind it, for every door; Care asks its own offer desk for an urgent visit, with every gate in place, only when the door offers one, and on this runtime is refused `service-not-offered`, because `services.json` places Thuso SOS in phase two and Care's seed phase is one. The press says in the contract's sentences that no ambulance partner is connected and, for an urgent-visit door, that no nurse is being asked because Thuso SOS is not offered on this build — a check holds that sentence to the phases in `services.json` and `care.json`, so it must be rewritten the day the phase moves — and the emergency numbers stay first on all three platforms and are the web's fallback while the pathway downloads. `POST /v1/safety/sos/{sosRef}/stand-down@1` takes one of the reasons on the screen and publishes `sos.stood_down@1`, which closes Core's concern with the outcome `closed-loop.json` maps it to and withdraws any Care offer nobody accepted. The desk reads `GET /v1/safety/sos@1` — never the patient, the answers or a plan — and the area only through `GET /v1/safety/sos/{sosRef}/area@1` inside its window; the tick drops the area from the store. Next of kin: `POST /v1/safety/next-of-kin@2` with consent to the version of the wording shown, for the one purpose `consent.json` allows the next-of-kin role and no longer than its ceiling; `GET /v1/safety/next-of-kin@1`; `POST …/withdraw@1` in one action; and `POST …/alert@2`, which records every attempt as not sent, with the reason, inside the `next-of-kin-alert-window` and `next-of-kin-alert-retries` the SOS was pressed under — three new Safety settings with proposed defaults and bounds in `field-safety.json`. Exactly one dispatch happens without a human, and `sos-press.json` `engine.dispatchWithoutAHuman` says which. `sos@1`, `next-of-kin@1`, `alert@1`, `checkins@1` and `sos.raised@1` are withdrawn; `checkins@1` rather than built, because Safety starts every visit timer from `appointment.in_progress@2`, and a second way to start one would be a timer with a caller-chosen duration. `nok.notified@1` stays declared, frozen as it was, and is published by nothing: it says next of kin were told, and nobody can be until an SMS provider is connected. **Refusals added:** wearable-alone, dispatch-without-a-human (a fall a phone noticed, with nobody pressing), sos-channel-unknown, sos-area-not-on-the-list, no-priority-by-plan, sos-carries-nothing-else, stand-down-without-reason, sos-already-stood-down, no-sos-of-yours, sos-list-takes-no-filter, area-not-chosen, location-kept-after-the-window, no-such-sos, nomination-without-consent, nomination-purpose-not-allowed, nominations-read-by-their-patient, no-nomination-of-yours, no-such-nomination, nomination-withdrawn, nomination-expired, nomination-not-for-this-sos, alert-after-stand-down, alert-window-closed, alert-tries-used, next-of-kin-see-clinical-detail and guardian-authority-not-proven.                                                                                                                    | All three                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Configuration: every engine's settings in one shape                           | **Built on the engine runtime and the web back office, 15 September 2026.** The founder instructed that open questions become admin settings: "All the questions you are asking please factor them as the admin setup on config so that they are dynamic." `packages/catalog/settings.json` gives every setting one shape — key, label, help, owner, a type (minutes, count, moneyCents, percentage, boolean, enum, text, roleList, schedule, list or record), a default with its provenance (decided, with who, when and why; or a proposal, with who and why), bounds or allowed values that carry the same provenance, a unit, who may change it, an optional clinical review, what a change reaches, and a guardrail written as values the rules must refuse — and each engine declares its own settings in its own contract. `packages/engines/src/settings` enforces the rules once, for the engine runtime and the web alike, and binds each engine's `GET /v1/<engine>/settings` and `POST /v1/<engine>/setting-changes`, and `POST /v1/<engine>/setting-reviews` for a setting that waits on a clinical review, with the history in the engine's own store. Safety's settings moved onto it at version two of both routes. Version one carried timings in minutes and nothing else, and stays declared exactly as it was frozen, withdrawn, with no callers and nothing answering it. Safety gained two settings that are not timings: who may change the field-safety settings — a role list only the admin changes, which always names somebody and never a nurse — and which window a phone that read an older one is given, which opens her panic either way. Care's offer expiry — the founder's ten minutes — is Care's first setting, with bounds of three to thirty minutes as a proposal; `GET /v1/care/settings@1` and `POST /v1/care/setting-changes@1` are built, and `POST /v1/care/setting-reviews@1` has been built since Care's clinical scope settings began waiting on a review (the row below). The refusals are the feature: a change says why, is made against the version in force and changes something; a value of the wrong kind, nought where the type demands more (asked before the bounds), a role off the vetting register, wording too long or without a sentence it must keep, a rota that leaves hours uncovered, or anything outside the bounds or allowed values is refused in the contract's sentence; only the role or capability a setting names changes it; a clinical review is confirmed by a holder of the capability, never by the person who made the change, once, against the value in force; the history is only appended to; and whatever has started — a visit, a panic, an offer already on a nurse's screen — keeps the settings version it started with. The back office's Configuration tab draws every setting from the contracts, with a renderer and an editor per type, a search, an engine filter and each setting's history; "Not clinically reviewed" shows wherever a review is waiting. The build fails when a setting leaves the shape, a default sits outside its own limits, a bound or a default loses its provenance, a role or capability does not exist, a guardrail's forbidden value is accepted, an engine with settings lacks its routes or their shared sentences, a history is edited, or an engine, a screen or an emitter types a setting's value or reads a default straight from a contract                                                                                                      | Engine (Safety, Care) and the web back office's Configuration tab; iOS and Android use the generated defaults, each saying whether it was decided                                        |
| Care's clinical scope settings, and a doctor's clinical review of a setting   | **Built on the engine runtime, the web back office and the doctor's workspace, 15 September 2026; iOS and Android use the generated defaults.** Four Care settings, each an integrator's proposal waiting on a clinical review by a holder of `sign-clinical-review`: who may be offered an injection, a family planning and a sick-note visit (`injection-roles`, `family-planning-roles`, `sick-note-roles` — role lists whose default is exactly what Care did, any registered nurse or locum, because no scope on the vetting register names these services yet; an admin may name only roles granted `take-visit`, at least one and at most both), and whether an Encounter entry counts as signed (`encounter-entry-counts-as-signed`, on by default, because no record route answers Care yet). The three service rows in `packages/catalog/care.json` name their setting instead of holding a role list, so the roles live in one place. `POST /v1/care/setting-reviews@1` is built through `settingsRoutes()`, and `GET /v1/care/settings@1` is read by the doctor who reviews as well as the admin who changes. Care matches with the role lists in force when it makes an offer and the offer keeps them; a visit keeps the encounter rule it started under. The Configuration tab shows "Not clinically reviewed" beside every value in force that no reviewer has confirmed, the default included; the nurse's offer card says it for an offer made under an unreviewed role list; and the doctor's Review queue carries a "Settings waiting for clinical review" panel with who put each value in force, from, to and why, and a confirmation with a reason. The refusals are the feature: a role the register does not let attend a visit — a carer, an operator, an admin, a courier — or no role at all is refused; a review confirms the exact version it names and never an older or a later one, and a change after a confirmation needs a new one; nobody confirms a change they made; a review without a reason, a second review of one value, a review of a setting that needs none, and a review by a nurse, an admin, an unnamed doctor or a doctor whose registration has lapsed are refused, each in the contract's sentence; and with the encounter rule off, handover and completion are refused in an engine sentence naming the record route that is missing, `GET /fhir/{resourceType}/{id}`. An unreviewed value never stops care: it is in force, and says so. A phone shows "Not clinically reviewed" only while the generated default itself is unreviewed, because it never uses the value an admin puts in force on the web                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Engine (Care), web back office (Configuration), web doctor's workspace (Review queue), web nurse offer card; iOS and Android offer cards and handover use the generated defaults         |
| Cancelling and moving a visit                                                 | Three states worked out from the visit's own start and the window declared in `packages/catalog/cancellation.json` — nothing types "2 hours" any more. The move is offered before the reasons, and again to whoever says the day is the problem; none of the four reasons asks a person to justify themselves and choosing none of them still cancels, recorded as "I would rather not say". Cancelling inside the window is never refused — it is recorded as late and costs nothing — and the one cancellation refused is one for a visit that has already begun, which belongs to the clinical record rather than a booking screen. A cancelled visit is kept under Cancelled with its reason; a moved one keeps its reference, person, address and service. No screen states a charge: what a late cancellation costs is recorded in the contract as an open legal and commercial decision. The window is refused as a literal in hand-written native source by `check-boundaries.mjs`, which is the check the contract was written for — the two hours had lived as a string in one Swift file and one Kotlin file with nothing to compare them against                                                                                                                                                                 |

| Teleconsultation call                                                         | The encounter between the review queue and the record. A roster naming everyone who can see and hear the patient — doctor on video, nurse in the room, guardian, interpreter — each consented to separately and each removable mid-call at a cost stated before the question. Recording asked as a second question and refused outright in the preview, with what it would keep, for how long and who could open it written down. A four-rung connection ladder where sound only is a designed path and what the doctor may conclude shrinks with the line. A dropped call held open for ninety seconds on both screens, with the doctor calling back. And seven encounter outcomes, worked out rather than chosen, of which five are not consultations and cannot write an assessment, a plan or a charge A queue that holds readings on the phone, and the four disagreements a queue actually produces: a duplicate, a stale write, a clock skew and a capturer whose standing lapsed between capture and arrival. Three of the four are settled by a clinician; only the clock one is settled by the server | Nurse earnings and payouts                                                    | A week's visits with what each one paid, four payout states including one the bank sent back, a reversal and a correction that each name the visit and the reason, the whole split of a visit — the nurse's share, the card fee and what MyThuso keeps — the tax-year total with a statement that nothing was withheld, and a payout-account change that re-verifies and then waits 48 hours. Not one visit amount is written down: a line names a service and the money is that service's nurse share in the catalogue the patient is quoted from | Prescription substitution and chronic authorisation                           | Three substitution classes rather than two, because section 22F of the Medicines and Related Substances Act 101 of 1965 does not permit generic substitution — it requires the pharmacist to tell the patient and dispense, with four exceptions. So the lowest class is _may be substituted, and the patient is told_; the middle one is the pharmacist's own judgement, carrying their name, SAPC registration and a written reason, with the prescriber informed; the third is _must not_, and an item in it carries no control at all rather than a disabled one. Five fictional items covering all four statutory exceptions and two clinical grounds — narrow therapeutic index, a patient stabilised on a product, a prescriber's own-hand _no substitution_, a biological, a supply failure and a patient who simply said no. Nothing can be marked handed over until the words said to the patient have actually been shown. A chronic authorisation boxed twice, by a date and by a number of repeats, ending on whichever comes first and then in a review; an early collection refused with a date and a question about the last month; and the expiry written down nowhere, worked out on all three platforms from the same 30.44-day month the vetting module renews credentials on | Employer and sponsor programme administration                                 | An employer's report is built by asking which counts may be published at all: a floor of twelve people, a dominance rule for the group where thirteen of fourteen answered the same way, secondary suppression so that nothing hidden can be had by subtracting the published groups from the total, and rounding to the nearest five so two reports laid side by side do not name whoever changed their mind. The published groups deliberately do not sum to the total and the screen says why. A suppressed group keeps its name and says why it is suppressed, because a blank reads as an error somebody goes and asks about. Declining is not a category anywhere: the difference between eligible and took part contains people who said no, people who have not got round to it and people who were away, and nothing separates them. Enrolment, and what leaving does and honestly cannot do. For a sponsor: a statement of what they paid for, defaulting to an amount and a date, with the service named only if the recipient switches it on for that sponsor | Localisation, and how much of it is real                                      | The shell, navigation, tab bar and primary actions in all eleven written official languages — English, isiZulu, Sesotho, Afrikaans, isiXhosa, Sepedi, Setswana, Xitsonga, siSwati, Tshivenḓa and isiNdebele. Ten of the eleven were drafted by software and read by nobody who speaks them, and every screen that offers one says so on the row where the choice is made rather than in a footnote. A language may only be presented as reviewed once the contract names the person who read it, the organisation and the day; the build fails otherwise. Four languages carry the hero banner and the vetting console as well, and which sets a language covers is declared rather than discovered | South African Sign Language                                                   | An official language since 2023 and a visual one, so it is deliberately **not** in the list of languages the interface is translated into — a toggle that changes nothing is a claim of access rather than access, and the build fails if it appears there. It is a communication requirement on the account, with the written language a separate choice; six things a visit and a call must do, and six that must never happen, including a family member used as the interpreter for a clinical conversation and a Deaf patient written to in English and assumed to have understood. The interpreter is the participant the teleconsultation roster already has — named, consented to and removable — rather than a second, quieter mechanism. None of it is a working service, and the screen says so | Colour contrast, computed                                                     | Thirty-two foreground/background pairs are computed from `packages/design-tokens/tokens.json` on every build against WCAG 2.2 AA, and none of them fails. Six once did and were parked with a measured replacement each; all six are now fixed rather than re-parked — `--faint`, `--amber` and `--danger` darkened to the values that had been measured beside them, and two teal-on-tint pairs reclassified, because they are icons at 3:1 and had been held to 4.5:1 as if they were label text. The build still refuses a parked failure whose proposed fix has not been measured, and one that has started passing and been left in the confessions. The focus indicator is two rings with tokens of its own: it was `--amber`, which also labels warnings, and darkening the warning enough to be readable took the ring to 2.2:1 on a dark panel. No single colour clears 3:1 against both a white card and a forest panel, so a light inner ring carries the dark grounds and a dark outer ring carries the light ones | The vetting vault, reachable                                                  | **Built and running** in `apps/api`. Nine routes reach the evidence vault and the gate: your own standing, another party's, enrolment, a document in, a document out, a decision, a second reviewer, a suspension and a restoration. Who is asking is never on the request — the account id comes off the session cookie and the role out of `vetting_parties` — so the refusals that were only ever true in a test suite are now true of a request: nobody decides their own vetting, a second reviewer is a different person, a standard-risk check will not take one, a check with no document on file cannot be verified, and a suspension has to say why. A reviewer whose own police clearance ran out overnight loses the vault this morning with nobody having to notice | Subject access, correction and the request queue                              | **Built** in `apps/api`. An export of everything the service holds about you, demanding a code, scoped to you because there is nowhere on the request to name anybody else, and written into the answer rather than into a file — so nothing expires and nothing is delivered anywhere. Correction as a right separate from deletion, with the mobile number refused because it _is_ the account. A queue of both section 24 rights with the thirty-day clock and an overdue count, holding ids and dates and no names, and a proof of response naming who answered and never editable afterwards | The security compromise register                                              | **Built** in `apps/api`. POPIA section 22, with no severity ladder — that question is a yes or a no, and a four-rung ladder in front of it is a way of answering it "medium". Any vetted party may report one whatever their standing, because a report you have to be in good standing to file is a report nobody files on the worst day. Nothing closes before it is contained, and nothing that reached personal information closes until both the Regulator and the people affected are recorded as told. Detection, paging and delivery are absent and the module says so is a separate, web-only preview                                                                                                  |
| Versioned consent, and the log of who opened a record                         | **Built** in `apps/api/src/consent`. Consent is to a _version_ of a _purpose_, never a boolean on a person: the fingerprint of the exact wording and of the withdrawal sentence is what is stored, so the words cannot be edited out from under a recorded consent; wording that has changed does not carry the old agreement forward, and the person is asked again with the reason for the change; withdrawal is one action with no reason required, is recorded as an entry of its own, and answers with what is kept anyway and the law that keeps it. The consents care depends on are separated from the optional ones structurally — an optional purpose has no route to the care decision at all. Beside it a clinical access log distinct from the sign-in log: who opened whose record, when, under what lawful basis and what capability, refused attempts included, readable in full by the person whose record it is. Records no reading, and has no column one could go in. Each of its entries hashes onto the one before it, and the head is sealed into the gate's keyed chain by the module that holds the key, so a row altered in the database file is detected rather than only correlated — `GET /health/access-log` says so, and names the first entry that does not follow | One Johannesburg, on real streets                                             | **Built.** The dispatch board used to be a 100×100 SVG square with circles on it, and the suburbs, their coordinates and the map window were typed into a React component, a SwiftUI view and a composable separately — three cities that had already drifted. There is one now: `packages/catalog/geography.json`, generated into Swift and Kotlin. The web renders it on Mapbox GL v3 with the base map's point-of-interest clutter turned off, and falls back to the schematic — the same zones and pins from the same contract — when no tile token is configured, which is the state every test runs in and the state the repository ships in. What the map refuses is the substance: a visit is drawn at the centre of its suburb and never at its address, no coordinate is sharper than three decimal places, the maximum zoom is 15 because at 16 a reader can pick out a house, a nurse not sharing a position is absent rather than placed somewhere plausible, no map draws where anybody has been, and nothing about the caseload is sent to a tile provider. Each of those is a boundary check that was proved by breaking it   | Web on tiles; iOS and Android draw the schematic, and neither declares a location permission                                                                                             |
| How a person reaches a doctor                                                 | **Designed and checked, on all three platforms.** The call itself was already built — who is in the room, what a bad line takes away, what an unfinished encounter may be called. What was missing was the module around it: the three routes in, the wait, and what may come out. The proposal sells no standalone video consultation, so none is invented — Thuso Doctor joins a nurse's visit, reads what was captured, or takes the booked counselling session that is already in the catalogue. The route a patient does not buy carries no price at all, because a nurse who has to justify the cost of a second opinion will sometimes not ask for one. The wait ends after fifteen minutes with a different plan rather than a longer wait. A call may write a prescription where a record exists, renew a chronic authorisation at the review that authorisation already ends with, and refer — and it may **not** issue a medical certificate or extend one, because a certificate says a doctor was satisfied somebody could not work and nobody was in the room. That last one is a question for the HPCSA rather than for this repository, and until it is answered the build fails if anybody flips it | GilbertOne, and push-to-talk on the phone                                     | **Built on all three platforms from one contract, 14 September 2026.** The drawing that used to refuse every microphone is now GilbertOne, on the founder's decision: `packages/catalog/assistant.json` holds the name and what GilbertOne is not, the six Pulse states and when each may be shown, the four situations, the suggested questions, a deterministic matcher — emergency words first, then the longest trigger phrase, and one honest answer to everything else, with the ambulance numbers and a nurse handover that says it was not sent — and eight Pulse events that carry no words and no audio. `scripts/emit-assistant.mjs` writes it into Swift and Kotlin. The web gets a text box. ~~and no microphone, because a browser's recogniser sends a voice to the browser's maker.~~ **Superseded on 18 September 2026**, and the reason was not overturned but answered: the live web assistant has a push-to-talk button in its composer, the browser's own recognition does the work, and what that may send to the company that makes the browser is disclosed on the screen before the first tap rather than kept quiet — see the two voice sections below. iOS and Android get tap-to-talk in English with on-device recognition only (`requiresOnDeviceRecognition` on iOS; `createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer` on Android 12 and later), live captions, a correction step before anything is sent, and the sphere's level driven by the live microphone while Listening. `voice` is a fourth capability state, `on-device`, and a twelfth locked door, `speech-transcript`, says what a South African speech provider must satisfy first. Refused: a diagnosis, a prescription or dose, a lowered emergency, an invented slot, any kept audio, Listening without the microphone or the microphone without Listening, being taken for a person or a doctor, and a crisis left to GilbertOne; and beside every conversation, that not recognising an emergency does not mean there is not one. **Fixed after the Wave 1 review, 14 September:** emergency words now match on stems with small gaps and folded diacritics, so "I have chest pains", "she bled" and "my chest feels tight" raise; a question answers on its own only if every other word is its trigger or filler, and anything left over gets "I can't assess the rest of what you said" with 10177, 112 and Thuso SOS — "Nothing needs you" is never said to a message GilbertOne did not read all of. The three matchers run one fixture list in the contract (Playwright, an iOS debug self-test, an Android JVM test). The descriptor is never shown without "not a person, and not a doctor"; the text fields turn off autocorrect and spell-check and say that the keyboard's own microphone is the keyboard's; iOS stops Listening on an audio interruption, a route change or an engine reconfiguration; and `pulse.utterance.finalised@1` and `pulse.escalation.started@1` are withdrawn for carrying group and question ids, replaced by @2 with booleans only. The emergency words are a draft marked `reviewedBy: null`. **Changed in Wave 3, 15 September:** the handover that said it was not sent now goes, when the person presses the button, to a simulated nurse queue that nobody reads, with a `SIM-HO-` reference, the ambulance numbers after it, and a summary of how they asked, what matched and an urgency that is never lowered — not their words and not which emergency words fired. | Core: the closed loop, the alert router and the Control Tower's read          | **Built on the engine runtime, 15 September 2026; the Control Tower's concerns board on the web since the escalation rota below.** `packages/catalog/closed-loop.json` holds the escalation ladder's five rungs and how long each has to be acknowledged, and the reasons an escalation and a snooze may give. All of them are proposals with `decidedBy: null`, cited from ThusoIQ Master v3.5 §13–15. `packages/engines/src/core` answers open, acknowledge, escalate and close a concern, raise an alert at version two (which adds the fallback version one could not carry; version one is withdrawn), the Control Tower's list, and Core's settings read and change. The clock moves an unacknowledged concern to its fallback, gives the fallback the owner's time, and then walks the escalation rota below. When nobody is left after its holder, the concern is exhausted: it stays open with the time, is first in the Control Tower whatever the filter, is never closed by the clock, snoozed or moved down a rung, and closes only with an outcome. A caller who asks to escalate further is refused with `no-fallback-left`, and the refusal keeps its audit row. Only the holder takes a concern on, and an engine acts only on concerns that came from it. An alert is one alert per engine and key while open, goes up a rung but never down, is refused if it carries any field beyond its shape (a reading could be hiding in one), and is snoozed only with a listed reason. The exhausted concern's announcement, `loop.exhausted@1` at the highest severity, is not declared, on purpose: no engine hears it yet, and the event contract refuses an event nobody reads. The Control Tower reads the concern first in its list, and the tick tries to announce it again until the event is declared. Core reads no Safety timing. The announcement's declaration is not built, and is said to be missing; the bus route, the protocol registry, its ratify route, the permission check and the audit export are built at Wave 6 — see the row below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Engine; web Control Tower (Incidents)                                                                                                                                                    |
| Core: the escalation rota as admin settings                                   | **Built on the engine runtime, the web back office and the web Control Tower, 15 September 2026.** Under the founder's instruction that open questions become admin settings, the rota the closed loop used to refuse to guess is two Core settings in `packages/catalog/closed-loop.json`, every default, bound and list length a proposal by the integrator with `decidedBy: null`. `escalation-rota` is a schedule of three posts, by role and never by name: the desk operator (held by `operator`, the Control Tower operator), on duty 06:00–14:00 and 14:00–22:00 every day; the on-call nurse lead (held by `nurse`), on call around the clock; and the Head of Operations, which no role on the vetting register holds, so the post has no role, says why, has no hours, and whatever reaches it is skipped rather than given to another role. The shared shape gained that: a post may be `role: null` with `roleMissing`, and no window may name it. `escalation-minutes` is five minutes at the desk and ten at the nurse lead, each an admin may set from one to sixty, exactly one fewer than the posts. The ladder's 5/15/15/5/5 minutes are how long an alert's owner has to acknowledge it by urgency, a different question, and are kept. The fallback is an extra rung before the rota, never rung one's override, so neither the engine's fallback nor the desk is lost; the last post is given the concern's own span. A concern keeps the rota, minutes and settings version it was opened under. The snooze and escalation reasons stay proposals rather than settings, because they are codes on the bus that subscribers and phones are built against. `GET /v1/core/loops`, `POST …/acknowledge` and `POST …/escalate` moved to version two, and version one of each is withdrawn: the list now says the holder, the rung, the post, the rota version, when it moves up and what it skipped; acknowledge admits the operator who holds the desk's post; and `no-fallback-left` no longer names a missing rota. `GET /v1/core/settings@1` and `POST /v1/core/setting-changes@1` are built through the shared settings code. The refusals are the feature: a rota that leaves an hour the desk or the nurse lead must cover uncovered, a window for a post nobody holds, a window that names a person, and a list of minutes that is not one per post but the last are refused in the contract's sentences; a post that is not on duty when a concern reaches it (the desk at two in the morning) is skipped and the skip written to `loop_audit`; an admin's change never reshuffles a concern already escalating; and a panic (`panic.raised@1`, which Core now hears) alerts every post on duty at once at the highest severity, walks nothing, reads no minute and is exhausted rather than walked when nobody takes it on — a rule, not a setting, and the build fails if a setting speaks of it. The back office edits the rota in the Configuration tab's schedule editor, which never offers a post nobody holds. The Control Tower's Incidents section lists every open concern with its rung, the post and role holding it, when it moves up and "exhausted" as before. Neither native app has a Control Tower or an admin surface, and no native screen shows escalation, so nothing is generated for them. Names of who holds each post, and the nurse lead's weekly rotation between people, are open: the settings shape has no assignment of a person to a post                                                                                   | Engine; web back office (Configuration) and Control Tower (Incidents); no native surface                                                                                                 |

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `apps/web/src/lib/gilbertone-bridge.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/Assistant.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/assistant.ts`                                                                          |
| GilbertOne, the API engine — one service, three clients                        | **Its own service at `apps/assistant-api`, installed dark by every deploy.** Not a feature of the web app and not a subcomponent of another service: a separate process bound to the box's loopback, reachable only through the nginx `location /assistant/`, with its address family authored in `packages/catalog/apis/assistant.json` — the thirteenth engine file, twelve addresses over twenty-six route versions, every one of them built, and every refusal sentence read from the contract rather than typed into a handler. The web is one client of it; iOS and Android each carry a typed client generated from the same contract and **ask it nothing yet**, because `capabilities.json`'s `unifiedApi` flag is `enabled: false` and generated `false` into Swift and Kotlin. Built is not the same as live, and five of the twelve are gated on a contract rather than a flag: triage's two steps on a register designating 0 of 12 protocols under a board that is not-formed and a Medical Director who is not-appointed, with a second lock in the source; the vital-sign reading on a DPIA, its real-device allowlist holding 0 devices; the handover submission on identity, roster and destination contracts that do not exist, so it is one unconditional 503 that reads no body and answers no 200. The 3 allowlisted external knowledge sources all ship `"active": false`, and the model tier stays dark until a provider is configured **and** `MYTHUSO_ASSISTANT_PRODUCTION=acknowledged` is written by hand. What never waits on any of it is the emergency: `packages/gilbertone` is the deterministic half, compiled into all three platforms, with no dependency, no network and no environment variable, and the build fails if it grows one. | `apps/assistant-api/src/server.ts`, `packages/catalog/apis/assistant.json`, `packages/gilbertone/src` |

### GilbertOne's connected-capability region on the live patient panel — 22 September 2026 (web only)

The patient panel now reaches the GilbertOne service's versioned routes beyond the turn the bridge
already carries. A new region sits beside the transcript — `apps/web/src/features/GilbertOneServices.tsx`,
through the one client `apps/web/src/lib/gilbertone-service.ts` — and offers, each on a press and
never on panel open: the truthful capability status (`GET /assistant/v1/status`), the sources an
answer may stand on (`POST /assistant/v1/knowledge/search`), a guided assessment (`POST /assistant/v1/triage/start`),
and the clinician handover (`POST /assistant/v1/handover/prepare` and `/submit`). The conversation's
own turn stays exactly as it was on `lib/gilbertone-bridge.ts`. This is web presentation, not a new
service or clinical contract: the deterministic engine, its frozen three-platform `Reply` union, the
session store, the voice routes, the knowledge federation and the triage gate are all untouched.

The region obeys one rule — every capability is either connected or it says why it is not, in the
service's own words. Guided triage is gated on a ratified protocol, so a health concern the
classifier could not place offers “Would a structured assessment help?” and, on acceptance, meets the
contract's `triage-not-ratified` sentence with an offer to prepare a nurse summary instead; no
assessment question or score is ever invented. Device readings are gated on a data protection impact
assessment, so the vitals row is a standing “Connect a device” explanation and a prepared pack with no
reading shows that explanation where the readings would be — never a simulated number. Handover
submission is gated on identity, roster and destination contracts, so “Send to a nurse” meets the
contract's `clinician-routing-not-built` sentence rather than acknowledging a handover into nothing,
and a session with nothing to hand over is refused with `nothing-to-hand-over`. Every refusal sentence
is read off the response by the client and rendered verbatim; where nothing answered at all, the region
says so in the catalogue's own “unreachable” words.

Knowledge results render as citations carrying each entry's id, title and source attribution, plus a
count of matches withheld for want of a source. **A truthful limit:** the federation holds a full
provenance object — authority, jurisdiction, evidence grade and dates — but the public
`knowledge/search` route's frozen v2 contract in `packages/catalog/apis/assistant.json` publishes only
`{id, title, source}`. Widening it is a version-three contract change with lock regeneration and native
emission, which is out of scope here, so the panel renders the attribution it is actually given and
fabricates no grade or date it was not handed.

The acknowledgment gate gained three disclosures — where your voice is processed, where answers come
from, and assessment and handover — as web-only copy in the lazy `packages/catalog/assistant-chat-ui.json`
(`service.consent`), not the frozen three-platform consent contract, because the microphone, the
knowledge federation and the gated triage and handover routes are this panel's alone. They are
disclosures, not tick-boxes: the microphone's moment-of-use consent is unchanged, and consent to an
assessment and to a handover is asked again by the control that uses it. Cloud `listen`/`speak` exist
but answer `speech-not-configured` at a 501 where no cloud voice is configured, so the browser's own
speech in `lib/voice.ts` remains the active path in this environment — the one file allowed to name a
speech API, unchanged here.

The region is a dynamic import of the already-lazy panel, so it and the routes it consumes are a
separate chunk (`GilbertOneServices`, 10.36 kB / 3.07 kB gzip) and add nothing to the patient entry.
Files: added `apps/web/src/lib/gilbertone-service.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/GilbertOneServices.tsx`
and `tests/gilbertone-services.spec.ts`; changed `apps/web/src/features/Assistant.tsx` (lazy region and
the three gate disclosures), `apps/web/src/features/assistant.css` (`.gos-*` styles) and
`packages/catalog/assistant-chat-ui.json` (`service` copy).

Validation: `npm run review-pack` regenerated the clinical review pack; `npm run check` passed (exit 0)
across every workspace including the web typecheck and the boundary scan; the web production build
passed; the assistant-api suite passed 233 with 0 failures; the new browser spec passed 12 (6 desktop,
6 mobile) covering the triage offer and its graceful refusal with the nurse door, the prepared handover
pack and its refused submission, the nothing-to-hand-over refusal, knowledge citations with their
attribution and withheld count, the truthful status reading and the three gate disclosures; and the
existing assistant journeys passed bar one pre-existing flake — the desktop run of `assistant.spec.ts:2009`
(“every reply is handed to the browser's voice”) intermittently misses its `A10` speech-cue attribute
when it runs inside the full suite, but passes deterministically in isolation on both viewports, and
still fails in-suite with this task's region render and gate disclosures both disabled, so it is an
ordering/timing flake in the shared checkout and not a regression from this work. The patient entry
measures **283,958 bytes
(277.30 kB) across 15 files** — the level-9 gzip sum of every script, module preload and stylesheet
`dist/index.html` references — unchanged by this work, against the last published 283,937 bytes and the
historical 282.16 kB budget the pre-existing excess above which remains.

### GilbertOne service answer waiting state — 22 September 2026

The founder reported the unmatched refusal appearing and being spoken before a real service answer.
The web panel now displays “Getting your answer…” while an unmatched turn awaits the service. The
fallback is retained internally and displayed once only if the service fails, times out, or returns
no usable answer. Recognised emergencies, matched answers and their refusal warnings remain
immediate; the emergency strip stays visible during the wait. Pending turns do not trigger speech
or an answer's facial cue. Closing the panel prevents a completed request from speaking off-screen.

Each response replaces only the turn object it was requested for. Out-of-order replies cannot
replace a newer question, and Start again invalidates pending requests before turn IDs are reused.
No server, model, refusal wording, clinical rules or timeout duration changed. Tests exercise delayed
success, failure, timeout, emergency interruption, reset and out-of-order completion.

Validation: `npm run check` and the web production build passed. All unit suites passed; the
assistant browser run passed 134 cases with four skipped and two reset-test locator failures.
After correcting the locator for the intentionally hidden welcome transcript, all ten focused
regressions passed on desktop and mobile, including those two reset cases. Initial patient assets
measure 283,935 bytes across 15 files (level-9 gzip), two bytes below the last published build.

### How to change GilbertOne's emergency terms

The words that make GilbertOne show the ambulance numbers are a versioned configuration, on the founder's decision of 14 September 2026: "propose it; we can configure as we go." Version 1 was the proposed starting list, accepted by the founder. Version 2, on 22 September 2026, is the one list in force on every surface — the web reads it directly, `packages/gilbertone`'s classifier reads it, and the emitters write it into the iOS and Android apps — and it added three terms that classifier's own typed list had carried all along ("heart pain", "pass out", "self harm"), because the list only ever raises and a term that raised yesterday must raise today. It is **not yet reviewed by a clinician — required before real patients**.

- **Where.** `packages/catalog/gilbert-emergency-terms.json`, under `groups`. Terms are lower-case letters and digits with single spaces; a term belongs to one group; a group maps to a condition in `packages/catalog/sos.json` or carries a name of its own. How a term is matched (stems, gaps, folded diacritics, reading everything) is in `packages/catalog/assistant.json` and is not changed here.
- **What to record.** Raise `version` by one. Add a `changelog` entry with that version, the day, who made the change by role, every term added and every term removed as `"group: term"`, why, and the new `termsHash` — `node scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` prints the hash of the list when it does not match. When a clinician reviews the list, fill in `clinicalReview.reviewedBy` and `reviewedOn`.
- **What must still pass.** `npm run check` replays the changelog and fails on a term changed without an entry, a removal nobody named, or a version and changelog that disagree. `npm run assistant` regenerates Swift and Kotlin. The shared fixtures in `assistant.json` must still pass on the web (Playwright), iOS (the debug self-test in `AssistantTests`) and Android (`GilbertFixturesTest`), so a change cannot silence a listed emergency. `falsePositives` — ordinary sentences the list raises, such as "I feel fit" — are reported by those tests and never block: tuning one out is a change to this file alone.
- **Decided, not edited.** The 30-second listening cap with tap-to-talk (`voice.listeningDecision`) and "Your Thuso AI Doctor · not a person, and not a doctor" (`identity.descriptorDecision`) are founder decisions of 14 September 2026; the build fails if the live value and the decision record disagree.

## What the new flows deliberately refuse to do

- A failed visit code stops the visit. It does not warn and continue.
- A vendor feed that carries more than was asked for is refused at the door rather than stripped on
  arrival, because a field that is stripped is a field that was received. A position feed may not
  carry a patient id, a roster may not say why somebody is off work, a pharmacy may not send back a
  diagnosis, a media session may not arrive with a recording, and a model may not return a
  prescription.
- Out-of-range readings are flagged for the nurse's attention and labelled as _not_ a validated early-warning score. Nothing is triaged automatically.
- A nurse assessment is never presented as a diagnosis, and a doctor cannot sign a decision without recording why.
- Abnormal laboratory results are held until a clinician releases them with an explanation.
- Paying for someone's care grants no clinical access. Scope, duration and identity verification are separate, explicit choices.
- Sexual and reproductive health, mental health and HIV-related entries stay hidden under every guardian scope.
- The dispatch map is decorative. Every dispatch action is available from the list, with a keyboard, and the map carries only a spoken summary.
- A high-risk check verified by one reviewer is not verified. The console refuses to let the same name second its own decision.
- A lapsed credential is not a warning. Dispatch, clinical sign-off, dispensing and result release are withdrawn by arithmetic on the expiry date, without anyone having to notice.
- Paying for care is not a permission. A sponsor is verified for payment and told, in writing, that it grants no clinical access.
- An employer is verified to run a programme and never receives a named result, under any circumstance.
- A protected category is never a header chip, for anyone. The header says a category is withheld, in the same words on every record, so a clinician knows to ask rather than assuming there is nothing to know.
- Finance sees a service code and an amount, never a diagnosis in words — and a protected service's line is withheld entirely, because a code is not anonymous to anyone holding the code book.
- Sharing a household is not consent, and paying for a member's care is not a permission.
- A conflict in the capture queue is never merged and never discarded automatically. A later timestamp does not win a
  clinical disagreement, and a retake is not the better reading: last-write-wins over two blood pressures is a silent
  clinical decision taken by a comparison operator.
- A reading never loses where it came from. A device reading, a nurse's hand entry and something the patient said are
  three different things on the screen at every step, and they stay different in the record.
- An overdue calibration does not block a reading — it labels it, permanently. An expired consumable does block it,
  because a strip past its date is not a measurement at all.
- Nothing is deducted from a nurse's share. The card fee and the doctor's review come out of MyThuso's quarter, and
  the whole split is shown — including what MyThuso keeps — because a marketplace that hides its own cut is asking
  to be guessed at.
- A suspension is not a confiscation. A lapsed check stops new visits reaching a nurse; the money for work already
  done goes out on its normal day. Earnings are never withheld as a sanction, because a platform that can do that
  makes every complaint a question of whether the complainant can afford to raise it.
- No tax is withheld, and MyThuso does not advise on it. Both are said on the screen, in those words.
- A deduction always names the visit it came from and the reason. There is no line that says only ‘adjustment’.
- Changing a payout account re-verifies the nurse and then waits, and a payout already in flight goes to the account
  it was authorised against — because account takeover is how a stolen sign-in becomes a stolen payout.
- MyThuso is not an ambulance service and is never arranged to look like one. The ambulance number is
  at the top of the emergency screen, above everything MyThuso sells, and no answer to any question
  moves it — because a screen that offers its own service first is asking a frightened person to
  compare the two, and some of them will choose wrong.
- Software does not triage. The emergency screen asks three questions and they route rather than
  assess: any one of eight named conditions — chest pain, difficulty breathing, uncontrolled
  bleeding, unresponsiveness, stroke signs, a seizure, a child under two who is floppy or not
  feeding, an obstetric emergency — ends the questions at an ambulance. Nothing is scored, and a
  condition in the contract that carried a severity would fail the build.
- Under 45 minutes is a target, not a guarantee, and it is never printed as an arrival estimate. An
  arrival the app cannot work out says it is estimating and says why, with the basis of every
  estimate beside it — the same rule the dispatch board follows.
- Urgency never relaxes vetting. A nurse whose police clearance lapsed is refused on the emergency
  screen in the register's own words, and there is no override there for anybody.
- Silence is not a cancellation. An unanswered callback keeps the request open and the nurse
  travelling; a dropped request is the one failure a panic button cannot be allowed to have.
- No fictional phone number may appear on the emergency pathway. Every other screen in this preview
  is fictional on purpose; that one is checked digit by digit against the numbers South Africa
  actually uses, and nothing in any app dials any of them.
- A consultation is not a private appointment with a doctor. Everyone who can see or hear the patient is
  named before the call opens, with where they are standing and what they can hear, and anyone except the
  doctor can be asked to leave mid-call without a reason. Nobody observes for training, ever.
- Consent to be treated is not consent to be recorded. The preview offers no recording and no switch for
  one — a control this build cannot honour would teach a patient to grant it — and says instead what a
  recording would be for, who could open it and that it would be destroyed after thirty days.
- A dropped call is not a finished one. The encounter stays open, the doctor calls back rather than the
  patient redialling, and an encounter that never reached a decision is written into the record as an
  interrupted encounter with no assessment, no plan and no signature. There is no button on any of the
  three platforms that would close it as a completed consultation, and it is never charged for.
- Sound only is not an error state. What a poor line takes away is not the patient's standing but what the
  doctor may conclude alone, and the screen names each thing withdrawn. A doctor on a screen examines
  nobody: anything felt or measured is the nurse's finding, under her registration.
- A doctor whose HPCSA registration has lapsed cannot open a call, and is refused in the clinical queue's
  own words rather than in a second sentence written for video.
- Consent is never a boolean on a person. It is to one version of one purpose, and when the wording
  changes the old agreement does not quietly become agreement to the new words — the person is asked
  again, and told what changed.
- What is stored as proof is the fingerprint of the exact words shown, not a pointer to wording
  somebody can edit afterwards.
- Withdrawal is never harder than agreeing was. One action, no reason, no confirmation step — and
  what withdrawing does not undo is on the screen before anything is touched, with the law that
  keeps it, because a withdrawal is not a deletion and saying only "done" tells the pleasant half.
- Refusing an optional consent cannot reach the care decision at all. Not "does not today" — an
  optional purpose has no route to it, and the build fails on a contract that says otherwise.
- Reading a notice is not agreeing to anything. The POPIA notification is recorded as an
  acknowledgement, cannot be withdrawn, and is never counted as a consent.
- A refused attempt to open a record is written down exactly as an allowed one. A log that only
  shows successes cannot show anybody an attempted intrusion.
- The access log records that a record was opened and never what was in it, and there is no column
  one could go in — the forbidden column words are read from the contract, not remembered.
- A log that only refuses to be edited by its own code is not evidence. Each entry hashes onto the one
  before it, and the head of that chain is sealed periodically into the gate's keyed chain by the one
  module that holds a key — so a row rewritten behind the service's back is detected rather than merely
  correlated, and the module that owns the log still cannot forge the chain it is evidence in.
- A substitution is a clinical decision, not a stock decision. An empty shelf is a reason to consider an
  alternative and never on its own a reason to hand one over, and an item that must not be substituted carries no
  control on the screen at all — a button that refuses is still a button somebody looks for a way around.
- A substitution never changes the molecule, the strength or the route. Those are the prescription, and changing one
  of them is prescribing done by somebody who is not a prescriber. The build fails if a fixture does it.
- Nothing is handed over as though it were what was written, and nothing can be marked handed over before the words
  said to the patient have been shown. A substitution is not anonymous either: it carries the pharmacist's name and
  SAPC registration, and a placeholder registration fails the build.
- A prescriber's own-hand "no substitution" has no override on the screen. The route back is the prescriber.
- No repeat is authorised by software. An authorisation runs out on a date and on a number of repeats, whichever
  comes first, and what follows is a review rather than a renewal. An early collection is refused with the date it
  may be collected, not filled quietly.
- An employer never receives a named result — not on request, not under a contract, not in an emergency, and not with
  the employee's own consent, because an employee who wants their employer to have something shares it themselves,
  from their own record, to a named person, for a stated purpose and period.
- No figure is published about a group of fewer than twelve people, or about a group where one answer covers four
  fifths of it, or in a shape from which a suppressed group can be recovered by subtraction. Where suppression would
  leave exactly one group hidden, a second is hidden as well. The floor is a judgement rather than a standard, it is
  written down so it can be argued with, and it has not been signed off by an Information Officer.
- There is no report, export, filter or support request that tells an employer who declined. Declining is not a state
  recorded against a name for an employer to be given.
- A sponsor cannot make payment conditional on being told what the care was for, and their statement names an amount
  and a date unless the recipient chooses otherwise — a line reading "sexual health screening" discloses more than
  most diagnoses do.
- Leaving a programme stops the counting from that day. It does not un-publish a figure already issued, and the
  screen says so rather than promising something nobody could keep.
- Nothing in any of these flows is transmitted, stored or acted upon.

## Delivered as build guarantees, 10 September 2026 — the fulfilment and safety seams, simulated

`capabilities.json` grew a third state. `absent` means nothing is behind a capability and the screen
says so; `connected` means a contracted supplier answers; `simulated` means a named stand-in runs in
this process so the product can be walked end to end before anybody has signed anything. **Not one
capability became connected, every `blockedBy` list is untouched, and a simulated capability keeps
its notice and changes what it says** — not "this is not switched on" but "this is simulated, and
here is what the simulation will not do".

Five of those stand-ins landed here: a pharmacy network, an interpreter roster, an acknowledgement
channel, a screening model and an instrument. None of them is reachable over HTTP. The eleven feed
routes go on refusing every payload, `decide()` still has no acceptance to construct, and a simulated
event enters in process through a function of its own — because a simulation flag on those routes
would have been exactly the condition `feeds/index.ts` spent a page arguing must not exist.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
