A dispensed item that does not carry the prescribed molecule at the prescribed strength is refused: changing either is prescribing, done by somebody who is not a prescriber. No step of the chain is produced without a pharmacist's registration on it — not an initial, not a branch, not "the pharmacy". The pharmacist's written reason and the words said to the patient never travel in the payload: both are about a person and a medicine, and this seam is not where a sentence about what somebody is taking arrives                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `apps/api/src/simulation/pharmacy.ts`, `apps/api/test/simulation-pharmacy.test.ts` (11 tests)                                                                                                           |
| **A simulated interpreter roster.** One interpreter's availability for one shift, and the two answers a visit gets: held, or dispatched                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | A family member or a child is never offered, and there is deliberately **no age threshold** — the contract reads "at no age", and a threshold is a number somebody argues has been met at eleven at night. What is checked instead is membership of the vetted roster, which nobody reaches without six checks and an accreditation. A visit that needs an interpreter and has none is held, not sent; there is no argument that produces a dispatch with an empty roster. | **A simulated acknowledgement channel — the one where working well is the hazard.** 10177, 112 and 10111 reach real people, and a convincing acknowledgement is exactly what would keep a frightened person looking at a phone instead of dialling                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | **It does not answer until the caller says all three real numbers have been shown**, and that refusal is the first line of the function — a simulator that worked out its answer before deciding whether to give it is one refactor away from giving it. **It cannot produce `en-route` or `arrived`**: both assert a vehicle in the world, nothing is coming, and neither can be made true by a fixture. **It carries no number.** Nothing may echo one of the three real numbers, and nothing the simulator composed may carry any run of digits, because a real number is replaced by a plausible one appearing beside it rather than by somebody deleting it. Its own instant may not precede the moment the numbers were shown, so "first" is arithmetic rather than a claim about a layout. And all three emergency screens now render the capability's notice **under** the ambulance block — iOS and Android rendered none at all until this, and neither did the Android prescription, dispensing, interpreting and kit screens or the iOS dispensing, prescription, interpreting and kit ones. It read green because `playwright.config.ts` reuses whatever is already listening on 5173 — its own comment says why the switch exists — so two full suites came back 333 and 346 passed on this tree without testing this tree. Set `MYTHUSO_PORT` when more than one checkout is live | No journey may type any clause of these five capabilities' notices, absent or simulated. Clause rather than sentence, because the assertion that survived the first sweep was `/Nothing is sent/` against a notice eight words longer. `tests/notices.ts` answers the same question the screens ask, so a journey no longer cares which state a capability is in. Scoped to these five: the other seams landed the same fix for their own capabilities, and a check covering all fifteen would fail on work that has not merged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `tests/notices.ts`, `tests/kit-capture.spec.ts`, `tests/deep-journeys.spec.ts`, `tests/patient-screens.spec.ts`, `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`                                                         |
| **Every refusal is enforced, and every enforcement is the contract's own sentence.** Held in both directions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | A simulator may not refuse in words `capabilities.json` has stopped using — each lookup must match exactly one sentence. And **no sentence in a capability's `simulation.refuses` may sit there unenforced**: a refusal written down and not enforced reads like a control and is a paragraph, which is the shape of every readiness claim that file exists to refuse. One refusal is not its simulator's to enforce — a TypeScript module cannot declare an Android permission — and that exemption is written down with the check that holds it named and asserted to still exist. ## Delivered as build guarantees, 10 September 2026 — the ingestion boundary

Fifteen capabilities are declared and none is connected. Most of them are blocked on something no
engineer can produce: a contracted SMS provider, a payment provider, a nurse roster, live device
positions, agreements with thirteen credentialing authorities, a pharmacy network, an interpreter
service, an ambulance partner, a media stack, an AI licence. Nobody can sign a contract from inside a
repository, and nothing here changes that. **Not one capability became connected, and reaching
`connected: true` is now strictly harder than it was.**

What landed is the seam each of those suppliers would arrive through, described as data and held by a
check, plus the door itself: eleven routes in `apps/api` that validate a payload, refuse it, record
that they refused, and answer with the capability's own not-connected sentence.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Where                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Eleven feed seams, one per supplier that would have to be signed.** Each says what would have to arrive as a schema derived from what the product already renders, points at the sample data standing in for it today rather than changing it, names the conditions that must be true before it may be switched on, and lists the fields that must never arrive at all | A capability may not be marked `connected` while any switch-on condition of any feed serving it is unmet — so the flag now needs the specific things somebody would otherwise satisfy in their head at eleven at night. No feed may accept a field another feed refuses by name. Every capability is either served by a seam or carries a written reason there is none: `voice` (the blocker is not a vendor but an unanswered question about recording somebody describing a symptom in their home), `devices` (`src/capture/**` already is the seam) and `clinical-records` (there must not be a route). A feed may not record its section 72 determination as made | `packages/catalog/feeds.json`, twenty checks in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`                           |
| **The doors themselves, all locked.** A route per feed, registered from the contract rather than written out, so a feed added next year cannot forget to be refused. `GET /feeds` is what a vendor would otherwise be sent as a PDF that goes out of date the week after                                                                                                 | Every route refuses every payload, **including a well-formed one** — the correct answer to a correct payload, from a service with no adapter behind it. `decide()` returns a refusal type with no success variant. A forbidden field is refused **before** the shape is checked and at every depth, on a canonical spelling, so a patient id in a position feed is caught as `patientId`, `patient_id`, `PATIENT-ID` or nested inside a wrapper. A capability marked connected makes the route throw rather than start accepting                                                                                                                                      | `apps/api/src/feeds/**`, `apps/api/test/feeds.test.ts` (21 tests)                                        |
| **Nothing a stranger typed is written down.** A JSON key is a string somebody else chose, and `{"bp 180 over 110 mmHg": 1}` is a valid one                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | A forbidden field is reported by **the contract's** name for it, never the sender's. A field that is simply not in the schema is reported as a **count**, never as a name. No value is read, kept, echoed or hashed. Asserted against the response body _and_ against the audit chain — the sharpest refusal in the work, because the ordinary way an audit trail becomes a second copy of a record is a free-text field nobody constrained                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `apps/api/src/feeds/index.ts`, `apps/api/test/feeds.test.ts`                                             |

A chain shorter than one witnessed is refused. A chain truncated to a prefix and grown again with real entries is refused on the hash at the witnessed position — the case `verify()` calls intact. A statement made over an empty chain vouches for nothing and is not blessed. The module may not reach a network, and may not lose the sentence saying it publishes nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `apps/api/src/protection/witness.ts`, `apps/api/test/witness.test.ts`                                    |
| **The one number in the service that is a proposal can now be measured.** Sixty writes per caller per fifteen minutes was arrived at by reasoning, not by watching anybody                                                                                                                                                                                               | `write_windows` may not grow a column somebody could be identified by — five integers per window and nothing to join to anything. `busiest` is declared a lower bound rather than presented as the figure, because the limiter slides and the bucket does not. ## Delivered as build guarantees, 9 September 2026

Three pieces of work with no screen of their own. Each turns a claim this repository was making in
prose into something that fails the build when it stops being true.

A range whose ends are the wrong way round is refused at generation, because it would flag every reading ever taken. A range stated without the sentence saying it is indicative and not a validated early-warning score is refused on every platform. An answer loads nothing, submits nothing and asks for no camera, microphone or location. HSTS is sent only where the cookie is already `Secure`, and never with `preload` — a preload entry is a submission to a list the service cannot withdraw itself from. A request is capped at fifteen seconds and its headers at five, so a connection sending one byte a minute can no longer hold a socket for Node's five                                                                                                               | `apps/api/src/server.ts`, eight tests in `apps/api/test/server.test.ts`, nine checks in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`                                                                  |

| **The authorisation gate got routes, and an actor nothing can lie about.** `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md` said the sharpest thing anybody has said about this codebase: the gate had a real vault, a real test suite and no HTTP route reaching either. The reason was written into `server.ts` — a route that takes an actor id off a request body and hands it to the gate is precisely the hole the gate exists to close | Nine routes now reach the vault, and every one resolves who is asking from the session cookie and the vetting register: the id is the signed-in account, the role is read out of `vetting_parties`, and the purpose is derived from whether the party being acted on is the caller. Nothing on a request can name any of the three. A reviewer whose own clearance lapsed overnight is refused this morning, over HTTP, by the same arithmetic that always refused her in the tests. The founding ceremony still has no route and a request to one is a 404 | `apps/api/src/actor.ts`, nine routes in `apps/api/src/server.ts`, thirteen journeys in `apps/api/test/vetting-routes.test.ts`, two checks in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |
| **Export, correction, the section 24 queue and a security compromise register.** Four of the controls the privacy document listed as absent, and the four that needed neither a vendor nor a key | An export that is scoped by having no id on the request, demands a code, goes through the gate, and is written into the answer rather than into a file — so there is nothing to expire and nothing to leak. Correction as a right of its own, refusing the mobile number because it is the account rather than a detail on it, and refusing the append-only logs while offering section 24(2)(c)'s note in the same breath. A queue holding both section 24 rights with the thirty-day clock, a proof of response nobody can edit, and a sentence saying plainly that it pages nobody. And a POPIA section 22 register with no severity ladder, no name and nowhere a name could go, which refuses to close an incident that reached personal information until **both** the Regulator and the people affected are recorded as told | `apps/api/src/subjectExport.ts`, `subjectRequests.ts`, `incidents.ts`; twenty-nine journeys in `apps/api/test/privacy-routes.test.ts`; six checks in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |
| **What a legitimate caller may do, decided.** The privacy document listed the unlimited routes and left them, with a reason that was correct: a limiter is a decision nobody had made | Sixty requests per caller per fifteen minutes, on every write and on the four health routes, keyed on the account where a session resolves and on the address where it does not. It is in the dispatcher rather than on each route, so a route added next year cannot forget it, and only the three sign-in routes — which carry tighter limits of their own — are exempt. **The number is a proposal**, arrived at from the busiest honest sequence this service can produce and labelled as such where it lives. And the three `/health/*` integrity routes now answer the loopback only, which is what the comment beside them always claimed | `limits.writesPerCallerPerWindow` in `apps/api/src/config.ts`, the dispatcher in `server.ts`, four checks in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |

And `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md` opens with an audit of its own controls: which are implemented,
which are written and unreachable, and which are prose. Of its twenty-six rows, eleven are now
implemented, five partial and six absent — up from six, four and twelve — and every absent one names
the obstacle rather than a shortage of time: a vendor, a key ceremony, an agreement, or a fact about
a person that no code can establish. Two of the four things it called libraries with test suites now
have routes. The clinical access log's `open()` and offline capture still do not, and their reasons
are now different from each other: one is waiting for a record to open, the other for a device this
service can identify rather than be told about. **`clinical-records` is not connected and nothing in
this increment changes that** — no clinical record exists, no route touches one, and the boundary
check that fails the build on a clinical table in `apps/api` still refuses one.

Two invariants and one negative result. Nothing here has a screen; each turns something the product
was doing on trust into something that fails the build when it stops being true.

All four were typed beside a list that could have counted them, all four were fixed by counting, and nothing stopped them coming back | No literal digit may appear in a workspace's metric strip on any of the three platforms — the shape all four lies had in common, and the only half of the invariant a source check can decide. A figure with no list beneath it to count (reviews finished today, a week's earnings) is excused **per file and per string**, because "18" was a doctor's finished reviews on one strip and "Available nurses 18" over a board of seven on another, and a blessing granted to a number rather than to a number in a place would have hidden one of the four. The figures still typed over a list that could count them are ratcheted: a new one fails, and so does fixing one without bringing the number down. Whether a _counted_ figure counts the right rows is not decidable from the source and is held by a journey instead — the strip read and the board counted, in a browser, on both viewports                                                                                                                | `METRIC_STRIPS`, `TYPED_FIGURES` and `FIGURE_QUARANTINE` in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`; `tests/workspace-counts.spec.ts` (six journeys)                                               |
| **What a store says when the disk refuses it is a contract.** `durability` already held what the offline queue survives; what it did not hold was the three sentences inside Android's `FileBook` about a phone with no room left, a disk that refuses without saying why, and a file that will not read back                                                                                                                                                                                           | Three failures rather than one, on the same two-field shape the stores use: `leaves` is outcome ids — behaviour, comparable between failures — and `says` is the sentence a screen renders. Every failure must leave `nothing-deleted` true, which is `queuedIsNotLost` applied to a disk rather than to a network. No two may leave the same set of outcomes true, because two sentences describing one aftermath are one sentence written twice — and what makes a full disk worth its own words is that the person holding the phone can clear it. A failure that says the work is not on the disk must also say it is still on the screen and that what was written before is untouched. A memory store may not offer to explain a disk failure that cannot happen to it. ## Delivered — the money and identity seams, simulated

Three stand-ins behind four capabilities, so the journey a person actually walks — sign in, pay for
a visit, be paid for one — can be walked before a single supplier has been signed. None of them is
reachable over HTTP: the eleven feed routes go on refusing every payload and a simulated event
enters in process through a function of its own. None of them makes a capability connected either;
`blockedBy` is untouched, because a simulator does not unblock anything and the SMS provider is
still unsigned on the day the simulated one works perfectly.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | The refusals it adds It **will not stamp a receipt with a reference that does not say it is simulated**. | **Four of the eleven seams have a simulated supplier behind them, and none of them is reachable over HTTP.** `booking`, `dispatch`, `credential-verification` and `teleconsultation` are blocked on a workforce roster, live positions from nurse devices, agreements with thirteen authorities and a media stack. A roster of nine fictional nurses, positions that move, answers from the thirteen and a session broker now stand in for those four, so the journey can be walked before anybody signs anything: a visit is booked against somebody, the board assigns her, the patient watches the distance close, and a consultation connects | Thirteen refusals, each enforced in the contract's own words and typed into no simulator — a refusal reaches the code as the slug of the sentence's own words, so rewording one in the contract stops the build rather than leaving a simulator refusing in language nobody says. A credential answer never confirms a check the gate resolves as lapsed and refuses to be asked whether it grants anything; roughly one enquiry in eight comes back unavailable, which is never agreement. A session is a session object: no camera, no microphone, no permission on either app, and a line that is always a rung of the ladder and never better than one. The roster refuses a nurse whose clearance ran out, one whose application is unfinished, one working outside phase one, and anybody who is not in the register at all. And every screen along the journey renders the _simulation_ notice rather than falling silent — the failure this state guards against is not a screen that lies, it is a screen that stops speaking because something answers now | `packages/catalog/roster.json`, `apps/api/src/simulation/{roster,positions,credentials,sessions}.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/roster.ts`, fifty-six `node:test` assertions in `apps/api/test/simulation-*.test.ts`, nineteen checks in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, `tests/simulated-care.spec.ts` |

The two screens that used to hold their own roster are held to reading the shared one. ## Delivered — Live well, the healthy-living tab, 12 September 2026 (web)

The founder's prototype carries a healthy-living tab beside Care, Visit, Passport and Family, and
`packages/catalog/wellbeing.json` is the whole design of it: five habits, ten refusals and a
`neverSoften` clause. This is that contract rendered. It is the only screen in MyThuso that talks to
somebody about their own body with no clinician in the room, and it is almost entirely refusals —
every harmful decision available here is a normal, well-intentioned product decision anywhere else.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Where                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A place to write down what you did, in your own words.** Five habits — moving, eating, sleeping, how you are, taking your medicines — each with the contract's own question, one chosen at a time so the field carries exactly one prompt. Five stacked boxes would have read as a form somebody is expected to complete, which makes four empty ones a judgement | **No field takes a number**, which is the single decision that makes the rest safe: there is nothing to total, average, compare or plot. Not steps, because a step count needs a device this product does not have; not calories, because counting is a clinical intervention for some people and an eating disorder's favourite tool for others, and this product cannot tell which one is reading. **No score, grade, target, streak, rank or percentage**, and the build reads the feature's own files for each of those words and for a digit with a unit on it. **No chart**: a chart of a body's week invites a line of best fit through somebody's life | `packages/catalog/wellbeing.json`, `apps/web/src/lib/wellbeing.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/Wellbeing.tsx`        |
| **A record, and the days nothing was written are not days at all.** What you wrote, when you wrote it, newest first, in day groups — and a day with nothing on it is absent rather than drawn as an empty row, an outline or a dash                                                                                                                                 | A run of days is never counted, coloured or compared. A streak that breaks punishes somebody for being ill, which is the population this product is for, and it says the same thing in a friendlier font as an empty row for a day somebody spent in bed. **Nothing congratulates and nothing corrects**: encouragement aimed at somebody whose condition is getting worse reads as a product that has not noticed, so silence is the default. What somebody wrote they can take back — a sentence about your own body you cannot delete is a file rather than a diary                                                                                         | `days()` in `apps/web/src/lib/wellbeing.ts`, `tests/wellbeing.spec.ts`                                           |
| **Bring it to your next visit — which is a prompt, not an integration.** The card opens the visit's own screen and what was written is rendered there, beside what to have ready                                                                                                                                                                                    | **Nothing is shared by default.** What a person writes is not added to the record and is not sent to a nurse or a doctor, in the contract's words, beside the field they are typing into. Nothing is copied into the record until a clinician records it as part of the visit — which is what the visit screen says rather than what it does, because there is nothing behind it to do                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `BroughtToTheVisit` in `apps/web/src/features/Wellbeing.tsx`, `VisitDetail` in `apps/web/src/features/Pages.tsx` |
| **It names `clinical-records`, deliberately.** There is no wellbeing supplier to be blocked on, and inventing one would be inventing a gap that does not exist                                                                                                                                                                                                      | The notice is the contract's sentence, word for word, and it is the one thing this screen most has to be honest about: nothing typed here is stored, and no real record exists behind it. Nothing is persisted — the entries live in React state for the length of the visit to the app, and the screen says so                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `capabilityId` in `apps/web/src/lib/wellbeing.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/NotConnected.tsx`                    |
| **Each refusal is rendered where it bites.** Seven together under "What MyThuso will not do here"; the other three beside the heading, beside the field and above the record                                                                                                                                                                                        | All ten render word for word from the contract and none is paraphrased. A refusal read at the foot of a page by somebody who has already typed is a refusal that arrived late, and a screen's own sentence about what it will not do is a sentence nobody checked                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `apps/web/src/features/Wellbeing.tsx`, `tests/wellbeing.spec.ts`                                                 |

It is a sidebar row between the Passport and the family, and the first row of the More hub on a
phone: the tab bar holds five 44px targets at 320px and a sixth would have taken the width from the
four a person navigates by. It is also the one patient screen with no figure on it — the rest of the
application is built around a large thin numeral with a small label under it, and there is nothing
here that could honestly be set in that type.

sign in to, and then asked the reader to choose a workspace anyway. The founder's instruction was to
stop drawing the wall: _"create auto logins for all the user roles instead of creating different
entry points… everything will start on the landing page."_

## Cross-platform consistency

The demo verification codes, the identity check-digit validation, what a nurse is paid — a visit's amount exists in one file and is derived everywhere else, including in the claim the public page makes about it — and the whole vetting table — thirteen roles, seventy-six checks, every issuing authority's credential format, every scope of practice and every refusal sentence word for word — are duplicated in three codebases by design — each app is genuinely native. `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` fails the build if any of them drift apart. The clinical reference ranges used to head that list and no longer do, for the reason the whole list exists: they lived in the `observations` array of `apps/web/src/features/Clinical.tsx` — a React component — and the boundary check compared the iOS and Android assessments against _that TSX file_, so two native apps were held to a screen. Seven numbers that decide whether a reading is put in front of a doctor are contract, not presentation. They are in `packages/catalog/records.json` now, beside the consultation section that says they are indicative; the web reads them through `lib/observations.ts`, the emitter writes them into `RecordsData.swift` and `RecordsData.kt`, and a check refuses any screen on any platform that types one. The locale table used to be on that list and is not any more: it is generated from `packages/catalog/locales.json` into Swift and Kotlin and read directly by the web, so the two disagreements the old check could not see — isiZulu calling vetting one thing on iOS and another on Android, and a button reading "Apply to join" on one and "Start an application" on the other — have nowhere left to come from. The substitution table, the programme floor and the interpreter roster joined the generated list on the same terms: `packages/catalog/dispensing.json`, `packages/catalog/programmes.json` and `packages/catalog/interpreting.json` are emitted into Swift and Kotlin and read directly by the web, and none of the three generators writes down a conclusion — no authorisation expiry, no suppressed cohort, and no wait — so the arithmetic that decides when a repeat stops, which groups an employer may be told about and when an interpreter could actually be there is done three times from the same numbers rather than once by a generator.

**Live, and what that changed.** https://mythuso.co.za went up on 10 September — five entries over
TLS on liqzar-server, beside five unrelated production sites that were checked before the deploy,
after it, and again after certbot rewrote nginx. The deploy refused its first real run and rolled
itself back, on thirty conflicting-server-name warnings that were all bidza.co.za's and predate this
product; it asks whose conflict it is now, and reports a co-tenant's rather than stopping on it.
Three ways it could have reached another site's config were closed, one of them arbitrary root
command execution proved by creating a file. The health-check timer is enabled and reports honestly
that it is not checking the identity service or the backups, because neither is enabled.

**The map draws real streets, and asks first.** MapLibre and OpenFreeMap need no key, so the swap
removed a vendor rather than adding one. Tiles are **off by default**: a request tells whoever
serves it which square of Johannesburg somebody is looking at, and for a patient that is roughly
which suburb she is in and roughly when a nurse came to her house. The disclosure sits beside the
switch before it is pressed, and the 1.1 km figure in it is recomputed by the build from the zoom
limit, which fails if the sentence disagrees. Measured: zero tile requests before pressing, eleven
after.

**Eleven seams, all locked.** For every capability blocked on a supplier nobody has signed,
`packages/catalog/feeds.json` says what must arrive, what happens while it does not, what must be
true before it may be switched on, and what must never arrive — a nurse's coordinate may not carry
a patient id, a settlement file may not name the service, a media session may not carry a recording
URL. `apps/api` serves a route per feed that refuses every payload including a well-formed one, and
nothing a stranger typed is written down: an undeclared field is recorded as a count, never a name.

**The landing page is the front door, and the hero is four banners drawn rather than shown.** The
founder supplied four finished 1774×887 compositions on 13 September — logo, eyebrow, two-tone
headline, paragraph, call to action, three trust marks, two floating cards, a counter and a
disclosure, all baked into pixels — and asked for the pieces loose so the app could draw them
itself. `packages/catalog/hero.json` is the words and the structure; `packages/banners` holds the
one thing that stays a picture. The reason is not tidiness: a picture of a headline is a headline
nobody can translate, nobody can tab to and no screen reader can read, and eleven locales carry this
product's interface. The call to action in each supplied image is a button, and as a JPEG it is a
rectangle that does nothing.

The hero's earlier cut carried three slides from the locale contract — `slide1`–`slide3` in
`packages/catalog/locales.json` — as a photograph and a white card. Those keys have not moved: the
patient app's Explore and both phones still render them. What changed is the public page.

| What changed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The refusals it keeps, and the ones it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Where                                                                                                                     |

Every word is read from `packages/catalog/hero.json` and none of it is typed into the component                                                                                                         | The headline's accent is the brand green at 3.19:1 on this ground, which is large text only and appears on the `<h1>` and nothing smaller. Both halves of a slide cross-fade on opacity in one grid cell, so each column is the height of its tallest slide in every frame of the change: no box moves, which is what made the last carousel on this page four flaky specs rather than the fact that it moved | It never rotates for a reader who has asked for less motion — the flag is never set, so there is nothing to stop and no pause control is drawn, and the arrows still work.
