| **Section 18A, as arithmetic rather than as an intention.** No medicine earns a point and no point comes off the price of one. The shop stocks none today, so this is trivially true — and stays true because a listing that ever slipped through would arrive inert | `isMedicine()` is consulted by the kernel rather than the catalogue being trusted, on all three platforms. There is no earning reason for a dose, a script, a refill or a course: paying somebody to take a drug is an incentive scheme attached to a medicine, and it pressures the person least able to argue | `packages/commerce/rewards.ts`, `scripts/emit-rewards.mjs`, `apps/ios/MyThuso/Models/Commerce.swift` |
| **A points ledger is not a clinical record.** Every earning reason declares what it `discloses` and what it `never` does, and the kernel writes that sentence rather than a caller's string. A row reads “that a visit happened, and its date” and never the reason for it | The reason for a visit is special personal information under POPIA, and a points history is read casually, shown to family and screenshotted — the worst container in the product for a diagnosis. A free-text note field is the hole this closes, checked in the source and again in the browser | `packages/catalog/rewards.json`, `packages/commerce/rewards.ts`, `tests/shop.spec.ts` |
| **Points are not money, enforced by absence.** No command withdraws, transfers, pays or charges, so there is nothing to call by mistake, and no field in the kernel has the shape of a card | A balance that can be cashed out is a deposit, and taking deposits is licensed. The nurse recognition track is worth nothing at any rate and no file in `packages/commerce` may even name a payout, dispatch or earnings — a second scoreboard that reached either would be a productivity target wearing a badge | `packages/commerce/types.ts`, `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |
| **A tier buys convenience, never care.** Exactly one input — points earned in the last twelve months — listed exhaustively in the contract so the check is about a list rather than a reader's judgement | No tier may name a protected category, a currency amount, or anything about being seen sooner. A balance that moves somebody up a clinical queue is care sold by loyalty rather than given by need, and in this country's geography a tier influenced by an area code is influenced by race | `packages/catalog/rewards.json`, `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |
| **Delivery reaches exactly as far as a nurse does.** The shop's zones are the coverage areas in `packages/catalog/geography.json`, not a second list | A shop that quietly accepts an order it cannot fulfil has sold a person a wait. The check caught its own author inventing a suburb | `packages/commerce/fixtures.ts`, `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |

## The clinical instrument deck reaches the phones

The web replaced the nurse's and the doctor's three flat tiles with a deck of instruments in the
last increment; both phones still drew the tiles. They draw the deck now, from the same arithmetic,
in the same order, with the same two sentences at the top of it.

| What changed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The refusals it keeps, and the ones it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Where                                                                                     |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A drawing per figure, and not one of them carries a number of its own.** The doctor gets a ring with one arc per case on the queue below and the flagged ones brighter _and_ thicker, a 270° dial of flagged against waiting, and one bar per row as long as that row has waited. The nurse gets her day drawn to scale — each visit as long as its own service takes, both ends labelled — a ring of her visits with the signed ones filled, and the completed weeks under her week's total | Nothing drawn may introduce a figure. Every mark is built from the same arithmetic as the numeral beside it, off the rows the section lists, so a reader who distrusts a picture can count the list and a reader who distrusts a numeral can count the picture           | `apps/ios/MyThuso/Features/ClinicalDeckView.swift`, `apps/android/.../ui/ClinicalDeck.kt` |
| **The last two invented figures are gone from the phones.** “18 reviewed today” over “Median 4 m 10 s” had no list on the doctor's screen to be counted from. The longest wait replaces them, which is the same queue sorted. The nurse's week is `Earnings.currentWeek.total` rather than the string `598`                                                                                                                                                                                    | `TYPED_FIGURES` in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` is empty. The list stays, so a figure added to it in future has to be argued for on the day rather than inherited, and the check still fails on a blessing nobody uses                                                 | `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, `apps/ios/MyThuso/Features/WorkspaceView.swift`           |
| **The same three cases now carry the same three waits on all three platforms.** TH-2041 was 24 minutes and routine on iOS, 26 and flagged on Android and 65 and flagged on the web; TH-2045 was 1 h 05 m, 74 minutes and 22 minutes. “Priority reviews” therefore read 1 on one phone and 2 everywhere else                                                                                                                                                                                    | Every figure had been counted correctly off rows that disagreed, which is the counted-figure rule arrived at from the other direction. The waiting _sentence_ is arithmetic on the minutes now on both phones, so a row and the figure above it cannot drift apart again | `apps/ios/MyThuso/Features/WorkspaceView.swift`, `apps/android/.../ui/AccountScreens.kt`  |
| **Reduced motion is the finished mark, immediately.** Each arc and line draws itself once on arrival; under Reduce Motion on iOS and a nought animator scale on Android the progress is already complete on the first painted frame. The `now` hairline on the nurse's day keeps ticking once a minute either way                                                                                                                                                                              | A reduced entrance is not a shorter one. No clinical value is animated into place — an arc is as long as the rows it counts before, during and after — and a clock that stopped when a reader asked for less movement would be lying to them instead                     | `apps/ios/MyThuso/Features/ClinicalDeckView.swift`, `apps/android/.../ui/ClinicalDeck.kt` |

## Delivered — the Core contracts, 14 September 2026 (seed plan, Phase 0)

The Full Scope's first principle is "freeze the Core event contracts first; every engine depends on
them". Three contracts land ahead of any screen that will use them, with no user interface of their
own yet — which is the order the plan asks for.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Where                                                                       |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Frozen event contracts.** 38 events across the engines — the appointment state machine, Verify's person and credential events, the Passport's record, consent and access events, the closed loop's alerts and the shop's orders — each with an owner, a payload of declared field types, the fields it must never carry and its subscribers. Other contracts contribute their own `events` arrays to the same shape | No identifying field travels on the bus (the subject is an opaque token, never an SA ID number); no transcript rides on any event; an alert never carries a clinical value; an engine does not subscribe to its own event; a frozen event cannot disappear or change shape — a change is a new version, and `events.lock` fails the build until it says so                                                                                                                                            | `packages/catalog/events.json`, `events.lock`, `scripts/emit-events.mjs`    |
| **Consent grants — who else may read, what, why and until when.** Eight recipient roles with default scopes taken from the master document's roles table, each with a default expiry                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Every grant expires; a sealed category is never in a default scope and needs an explicit tick; a scheme receives aggregate data only, and no grant type exists for an insurer, employer or advertiser to see a person; revocation has no grace period; a responder's grant ends with the trip. No grant lasts longer than ninety days, for any role — the founder's decision of 14 September 2026, held once in `grants.maximumExpiryDays`; next of kin and a scheme came down from a year to meet it | `packages/catalog/consent.json` `grants`, `scripts/emit-consent-grants.mjs` |
| **The protocol registry, with nothing in it.** The twelve launch protocols are registered as drafts with no signatory                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | A protocol is not ratified without a named role and a date; a draft carries no content, threshold, dose or digit of any kind; nothing may cite a protocol version the registry does not hold. Clinical content arrives from the Medical Director and the governance board, not from software                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `packages/catalog/protocols.json`, `scripts/emit-protocols.mjs`             |
| **The app icon leaves the retired palette.** The teal-to-indigo gradient with a mango pulse becomes the wordmark's green into ink, with the pulse in lime because coral all but disappears against the green                                                                                                                                                                                                          | The iOS square is still generated from the one drawing and the Android launcher is still held to it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `packages/illustrations/app-icon.svg`, `scripts/emit-appicon.py`            |

## Delivered — Verify's gates, Trust Score v1 and the Passport's own service, 14 September 2026 (seed plan, Phase 0)

"Verify and Passport foundations go live before the first patient visit; no exceptions." Neither is
live — both are built so that going live is a matter of the controls they name, not of more code.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The seven onboarding gates.** Apply, identity, credentials, background, assess, train, activate — each with what happens, a fail rule and the sentence the person is shown. Every one of the 76 checks names its gate, and a role with no check at a gate says why. A party's status reads "Gate n of 7 — name" on web, iOS and Android, worked out from the checks on every read and never stored                                                                                                                                                         | A declined identity or background check stops the person at that gate and no later gate is reached; a lapsed check holds them at its gate with a lapse sentence, never the background bar; activation is refused until gates 1–6 pass. The national sex-offender and child-protection registers are not yet checks of their own, and consent to vet is not yet a record — both written down as gaps                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `packages/catalog/vetting.json` `gates`, `apps/api/src/vetting/gates.ts`                    |
| **Trust Score v1 — no score, no dispatch.** Hard gates only: a failing gate, a suspension or a decline is 0 and offline; somebody short of gate 7 has no score; everybody else is Verified. Dispatch ranks by distance then tier, and lists the withheld beside the ranking with the contract's sentence rather than last in it                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | No soft input has a weight until the Clinical Governance Lead decides one, so Trusted and Senior each say why they are unavailable; a patient sees a badge and never a number; a ranked entry carries the badge and the distance and nothing else                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `packages/catalog/trust.json`, `apps/api/src/simulation/roster.ts`                          |
| **The Health Passport P0, as its own service.** Its own SQLite file and master key, per-patient and per-sealed-category data keys wrapped under it, FHIR-shaped resources with provenance on every write, a consent gateway reading the grant roles from `consent.json`, break-glass and a hash-chained audit at `/audit/mine`. 44 tests                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | No grant, an expired, revoked or altered grant, the wrong purpose or a category out of scope is refused with a sentence; sealed content is excluded unless the grant ticks it in, and a clinician is told only that it exists; a scheme never receives an identifiable record; break-glass opens the emergency summary alone and flags a 24-hour review; it refuses a key shared with or derived from the identity service's; it will not start without the development flag, answers only on loopback, and nothing in `deploy/` may name it until a DPIA is signed                                                                                                                                                                                                | `apps/passport`, `packages/catalog/passport-gateway.json`, `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md`   |
| **What each grant role may do at the gateway lives on the role.** The Passport's roles came from a fixture while the contract was written; at integration the fixture was deleted and reads, writes, sealed-tick and clinician moved onto each role in `consent.json`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Only clinicians write; reading aggregates and being a non-identifiable role agree; a grant bound to a trip opens the emergency summary alone — one list read by the grant sheet, the gateway and the checks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `packages/catalog/consent.json`, `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`                             |
| **What the Wave 1 review found, and what changed.** A caregiver's grant for vital signs with the sealed tick opened a reading the patient had marked private; break-glass took its role from the request body and wrote a free-text justification — a name, a phone number, an address — into the chain the patient reads; provenance sat in the clear beside sealed entries; refusals decided before a requester was known were never audited; grants had no ceiling. Each is fixed and held by a test that fails without it; the Passport now has 60 tests | The sealed tick names what it opens, and a tick naming nothing is refused; private entries open for no grant in P0; break-glass identifies its operator by credential, records a reason from the contract and seals the screened note beside the review, never in the chain; every refusal is audited, and a probe of another patient's record is logged against them too; the Host must be loopback; sessions expire; identity-shaped fields are refused at any depth. Every grant role has a `maxExpiryDays` ceiling, equal to its default and never above the founder's contract-wide ninety days (decided 14 September 2026), which the gateway enforces on top of the role's own; and a list of `allowedPurposes`, which still awaits the Information Officer | `apps/passport/src/gateway.ts`, `operator.ts`, `screen.ts`, `packages/catalog/consent.json` |
| **Two frozen events carried what they should not, and were withdrawn rather than edited.** `person.trust_updated@1` put the Trust Score's number on the bus to dispatch; `passport.consent.granted@1` and `revoked@1` told the scheme-facing engine a patient's grant scope and sealed tick                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The @2 versions carry a badge tier and hard-gate status, and no scope or sealed flag; no engine serving only a non-identifiable role may publish or hear a consent event; score, points, rank, rating and their aliases are refused in any event from any contract; a withdrawn version keeps its lock line, loses every subscriber and may not be named in code; the lock fingerprint includes the type, and the build refuses a contract that is not frozen                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `packages/catalog/events.json`, `events.lock`, `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`               |

## Delivered — every engine's API contract, 14 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 2)

The documents name endpoints for one engine — the Health Passport, in §26 — and describe the rest as
capabilities, message sets and state machines. Before any more screens are built against a guess,
every engine now has a frozen contract for what it exposes, a generated client on all three platforms
and a mock that answers and refuses as the contract says. The conventions were delegated by the
founder and are recorded as awaiting a Head of Engineering.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Where                                                    |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| **148 routes across twelve engines, frozen.** Core 10, Access 38, Care 13, Clinical 7, Safety 15, Movement 12, Trust 15, Record 13, Medicines 9, Devices 8, Money 8; GilbertOne Pulse has none of its own and says why. 57 are built — the identity service's and the Passport P0's, mapped to the handlers that answer them — and 91 are proposed. 10 have an endpoint the documents name (§26), 61 serve a capability the documents name, and 77 are ours. *Superseded 22 September 2026, and the record is kept as it was written*: thirteen engine files now, and the boundary check reports **350** route versions — the assistant engine's own twelve among them, and every one of those twelve built. GilbertOne Pulse still has none of its own, and still says why | Every route names its callers, its purpose, at least one refusal and the events it emits; money and dispatch writes carry an idempotency key; no request or response carries a Trust Score, a transcript, a card number, an identity number or a sealed category; no engine reaches another engine's store except through the Passport gateway or GilbertOne's tool gateway; clinical resources live only in the record engine; a route claims `built` only with its handler as evidence, and an endpoint only where the documents give the path | `packages/catalog/apis.json`, `apis/*.json`, `apis.lock` |
| **59 events for the engines that had none.** Clinical, Safety, Movement, Medicines, Devices, Access bookings and Money's payments, payouts and claims — including §15C's admission sequence and §26's share-link, anomaly and discharge events, word for word                                                                                                                                                                                               | Every event obeys the existing never-lists and routing rules; a Sentinel level is a `rung`, not a tier, so it cannot be mistaken for a trust word                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `packages/catalog/events.json`, `events.lock`            |
| **Typed clients and a contract mock.** Route constants and request and response shapes are generated into TypeScript, Swift and Kotlin; `packages/mock-api` answers every route from fixtures, with a contract test per route (450 tests)                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The mock binds to loopback, refuses to start without its development flag and may not be named in `deploy/`; it refuses exactly as each route declares, so a screen built against it meets the real refusals first. Supplier callbacks — WhatsApp, USSD, HL7 v2, lab results, device readings, KYC and AFIS answers, responder positions — are doors still to be added to `feeds.json`, not routes                                                                                                                                               | `scripts/emit-apis.mjs`, `packages/mock-api`             |

## Delivered — Care's events and routes at version two, 15 September 2026 (Wave 3 gaps, Phase B, Care events lead)

The Wave 3 leads reported what Care's frozen versions could not carry. Each correction is a new version with its lock
lines, and each version it replaces is withdrawn: kept in its contract with the day, the reason and its correction, with
no subscriber and no caller, and its narrowing recorded. Nothing here is a real service: the engines answer on loopback
with synthetic data, no nurse is offered anything, and the patient told about a named nurse is told on a web preview.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`appointment.booked@2` and `appointment.in_progress@2` carry `serviceId`; `appointment.completed@2` carries `bookingRef`.** Every subscriber moved: Core, the record and Money to booked@2; Core, the record and Safety to in_progress@2; Core, the record, Verify and Access to completed@2. Version one of each is withdrawn. Access closes a visit's thread by the `bookingRef` completion carries, and the `careAppointmentRef` naming rule in `booking.json` and its coupling check are retired, not retargeted                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Nothing hears or publishes a withdrawn appointment event, and a subscriber move left half done fails the build; Care writes the version at every visit event, so a default can never publish a withdrawn one; a completion with no booking closes nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `events.json`, `events.lock`, `care/domain/visits.ts`, `offers.ts`, `care/engine.ts`, `access/engine.ts`, `money/engine.ts`, `safety/engine.ts`, `booking.json`, `apis/core.json` (Core's caller justification follows the event)                    |
| **Safety times visits by service on the runtime, and the desk works timers and panics through routes.** Safety keeps `serviceId` from `appointment.in_progress@2` and starts the visit's timer from that service's duration in `services.json` with the grace, steps and ceiling in force, and the timer keeps the settings version it started under; it hears `appointment.completed@2` (Safety added as a subscriber) and closes the timer; the clock emits `checkin.overdue@1` once per deadline. The nurse reads her timer (`GET /v1/safety/checkins@1`), extends with a reason (`…/extend@2`, which withdraws `extend@1`), says "I am safe" (`…/safe@1`) and checks out (`…/close@2`, which withdraws `close@1`). The desk — the `operator` role — picks up and closes an overdue (`/v1/safety/overdue-checkins/{checkinRef}/pick-up@1`, `…/close@1`), picks up and resolves a panic (`/v1/safety/panics/{panicRef}/pick-up@1`, `…/resolve@1`), reads a panic's position (`…/position@1`) and the queue (`GET /v1/safety/desk-queue@1`). Resolving publishes `panic.resolved@1` — the panic, the outcome's code and when sharing ended — and Core closes the concern it opened for that panic with it. Closing an overdue publishes nothing, because Core opens no concern for an overdue check-in and no engine acts on a close. Every offer keeps the answer beside its settings version. `offers@1` is withdrawn, and its `visit-zone-unknown` reasoning, which still said the booking carried no zone, is corrected in version two. ## Delivered — a route's refusals, callers and inner shapes frozen beside apis.lock, 15 September 2026 (Wave 3, Platform contracts)

`apis.lock` fingerprints a route's method, path, version and top-level fields, and nothing else. A
settings migration changed a live, pushed route's inner rows and refusal sentences under an unchanged
fingerprint, and only a person reading the diff caught it. Changing the fingerprint would have rewritten
every line in the lock, so its arithmetic is left alone and what it never covered is frozen beside it,
seeded once from the tree as it stood. Nothing here is a service: the locks are build checks on a contract
answered only by the development mock and the development engine runtime.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Three append-only locks beside `apis.lock`.** `apis.refusals.lock` hashes each of 175 route versions' own refusals — id, status and sentence, never the why — with the engine refusals that name it, and 99 shared refusals by scope and id. `apis.callers.lock` writes out each route version's callers, purposes and engine justifications. `apis.shapes.lock` hashes every field at every depth for a route that declares its objects, and seals the 59 route versions whose objects were prose on the day | A refusal added, removed or reworded on a frozen route, withdrawn routes included, is a new version. A shared refusal keeps its words while it is declared — different words are a different id — and a settings refusal changes only with a new version of the settings route shape. A caller, purpose or justification added is a new version; a narrowing is appended by `npm run apis -- --record-narrowing`, and the narrowest line is in force so nothing taken away drifts back. A committed line in any API lock or `events.lock` that is missing from the tree fails the build, compared with git from `lockHistory.from` | `scripts/api-locks.mjs`, `packages/catalog/apis.refusals.lock`, `apis.callers.lock`, `apis.shapes.lock`, `apis.json` (`lockHistory`, `objectFields.innerShapes`, `proseOnlyObjects`, six rules), the locks part of the API section of `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |
| **An engine refusal names the routes that answer it.** Core's `no-such-loop` and `not-your-concern`, Care's `encounter-signature-unconfirmed` and Money's `charge-without-a-key`, `payable-not-priced` and `scheme-sees-a-payment` carry `answeredBy`, ten routes traced from their handlers                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | The runtime answers an engine refusal only from a route it names, and faults otherwise, so a refusal cannot reach a frozen route by being declared for the whole engine; the names are in each route's refusal hash. A route that declares the same id itself is refused, because its sentence would win                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `packages/catalog/apis/core.json`, `care.json`, `money.json`, `packages/engines/src/runtime/runtime.ts`, `runtime.test.ts`                                                                                                                                           |
| **Inner shapes are data.** Every engine's settings read declares its settings and history rows in `settings.json` and word for word in its five live read routes, taking a value's, a limit's and a provenance's shape from the `settings.json` sections that decide them; those five leave the excused list, which is 54 now and only shrinks                                                                                                                                                                  | A new route with an object described in prose is refused, and so is a new entry on `proseOnlyObjects`. A field's name rules hold inside objects too. The runtime refuses a handler's answer with a key nobody declared, a declared key missing, null where null is not declared or a limit the contract does not name, at every depth                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `packages/catalog/settings.json`, `packages/catalog/apis/*.json`, `packages/engines/src/runtime/runtime.ts`, `contract.ts`                                                                                                                                           |
| **One command for a new route version.** `npm run apis -- --seed-new` appends a line for every route version and shared refusal that has none, in all four API locks, and never rewrites one; every failure that a missing line causes names it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Emitting the clients never appends a line, and appending never regenerates a client, so running the generators freezes nothing by accident                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `scripts/emit-apis.mjs`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

## Delivered — Money's events and routes, 15 September 2026 (Wave 3 gaps, Phase C, Money events lead)

The Wave 3 leads reported what Money's frozen versions could not carry. The payable now has its price from the
moment Care holds the visit, a refund is told on the bus, and the cash code's two proposed routes are built on the
runtime at version two, with version one of each withdrawn. Nothing here is a real service: no card is charged or
refunded, no cash changes hands, and the engines answer on loopback with synthetic data.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A payable is priced from `appointment.booked@2`'s `serviceId`.** Money opens `PB-<appointmentRef>` at the price `services.json` holds for the service, as it opens, rather than waiting for `visit.billable@1`. `booking.confirmed@2` is read for its service and opens no payable of its own. `payable-not-priced` keeps its id, status and sentence; only its reasoning is corrected, because a store opened under the withdrawn `booked@1` can still hold an unpriced payable                                                                   | A service the catalogue does not sell is refused on delivery and opens nothing; an amount that is not the catalogue's is refused before any visit is billable; one visit is one payable, because no event Money hears names a booking and its appointment together                                                                                                                                                  | `apis/money.json`, `money/domain/ledger.ts`, `money/engine.test.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **`payment.refunded@1`, owned by Money.** Published once when a cancellation's reversal comes back through the payment-result door: `paymentRef`, `payableRef`, `amountCents` and, when the payable was opened against a booking, `bookingRef`. The whole payment goes back, because `cancellation.json`'s late-cancellation charge is a decision nobody has made. Its one subscriber is **Access**, which marks its own booking refunded by the `bookingRef` and draws "Refunded" with Money's amount under a cancelled booking                     | Never carries card data, the cash code or clinical detail (`neverCarries`: `cardNumber`, `cashCode`, `findings`); a redelivered cancellation or a repeated reversal publishes nothing; a refund with no booking, or for a booking Access does not hold, marks nothing. Core is not a subscriber: nothing in Core acts on a refund                                                                                   | `events.json`, `events.lock`, `booking.json`, `access/domain/booking.ts`, `access/engine.ts`, `BookingStatus.tsx`                                                                                                                                    |
| **`POST /v1/money/payments/{paymentRef}/cash-code@2`, built.** The nurse who held the visit enters the patient's code at completion — on the web, iOS and Android — after the patient's simulated screen has shown it once. Wrong codes count toward `attemptLimit`, and the count and the audit row survive each refusal through `keptOnRefusal` on `cash_codes` and `cash_audit`. The code is declared a `secretRequestFields` entry, so the runtime keeps no digest of it in the replay table. `cash-code@1` is withdrawn, its narrowing recorded | New on this version: `payable-not-found` for a reference to no cash payment, and `cash-code-not-your-visit` for another nurse, both refused before a code is compared and so counting nothing. After the limit even the right code is refused with `cash-code-held`; the audit records who and when, never what was entered; every wrong code counts whatever key it came with, because a refusal is never replayed | `apis/money.json`, `apis.lock` and its three locks, `money/engine.ts`, `runtime/runtime.ts`, `CashCode.tsx`, `CareVisitView.swift`, `CareVisitScreens.kt`, `Money.swift`, `Money.kt`, `cash-code-on-the-runtime.test.ts`, `tests/money-cash.spec.ts` |
| **`POST /v1/money/payments/{paymentRef}/release@2`, built, and the desk's panel.** The operations desk (`ops-desk`, the caller `apis.json` names) releases a held cash payment with a `reasonCode` from `money.json`'s `cash.releaseReasons`; the hold and the count reset and the audit row says who, when and why. The back office's Control Tower lists held cash payments under Incidents with a Release action. `release@1` is withdrawn                                                                                                        | `release-without-a-reason`, `release-reason-not-known` and `cash-code-not-held`, each leaving the hold where it was; a release pays nothing and changes no code. The vetting register holds no capability for releasing a hold, so none is claimed and no other role is widened                                                                                                                                     | `apis/money.json`, `money.json`, `money/domain/ledger.ts`, `lib/cash-codes.ts`, `CashCode.tsx`, `StaffShell.tsx`                                                                                                                                     |

Still open: **no payable on the runtime carries a `bookingRef` yet**, because no event Money hears names a booking and
its appointment together — so on the runtime Access's refund hearing marks nothing, and `booking.cancelled@1` refunds
nothing; the web preview has no flow that cancels a paid booking, so the Refunded line is drawn only for a booking
that carries a refund. The phones show the cash-code entry and its refusals, and a payment held there stays held,
because the desk's release is on the web only. No route lists held cash payments for the desk on the runtime; the
panel runs Money's ledger in the browser.

to a capability either role was granted. Nothing here is a real service: nobody is vetted into either role,

## Delivered — governance packs for the people who must sign, 15 September 2026 (Wave 3, Governance documentation)

The founder asked for a Clinical Governance Lead to review the clinical defaults, and for a DPIA, an Information
Officer, a data residency decision and key custody before anything touches real patients. None of those can be
produced by code. What landed is everything those people need, so that their work is to review, decide and sign.
Every document says at the top that it is a draft with no effect until the named person signs, and every sign-off
field is blank.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                  | Where                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The clinical review pack, generated.** Every setting with `reviewRequired`, every unratified protocol, GilbertOne's emergency terms, the clinical scopes and proposed clinical roles on the vetting register, clinical proposals and safety numbers nobody clinical has decided, and clinical content with no sign-off recorded — each with its value read from the contract, a question and blank sign-off fields, and a list of what was looked at and left out | A stale pack fails `npm run check`; the generator never writes a reviewer, a decision or a date; a setting or proposal it has not classified is listed under "Not yet classified" rather than dropped | `scripts/emit-clinical-review-pack.mjs`, `docs/governance/CLINICAL-REVIEW-PACK.md`, `npm run review-pack` (also in `generate`), the `generated` list in `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs` |
| **A DPIA draft**, an **Information Officer checklist**, and decision papers on **data residency** and **key custody**, with a README giving the order and what the app refuses until each is signed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Each asks the legal questions rather than answering them, cites a repository path for every claim about the system, and refers to a contract rather than restating its numbers                        | `docs/governance/`                                                                                                                                                                     |

Still open: every decision in those documents. Two things the work found are written into them rather than
decided. Only the doctor role holds `sign-clinical-review`, so a Clinical Governance Lead who is a registered nurse
could not confirm a setting through the doctor workspace's panel. And the DPIA draft asks whether police clearance
and face-match information are special personal information under POPIA section 26, which
`docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md` currently treats as outside the special category.

## Delivered — Medicines & Labs on the engine runtime, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 4, Medicines & Labs)

A prescription, the check it stands on, the bag it leaves the pharmacy in and a lab result a clinician must
acknowledge, built as Medicines' routes on `packages/engines` with Clinical's acknowledgement and Core's concern
around it. **Where the content lives:** Medicines holds references and states and never a medicine, a dose or a
result — the prescriber writes the MedicationRequest and the laboratory the result into the Health Passport
through the consent gateway, and the engine's store keeps the entry references, who acted, the schedule the
custody rule needs and the state (`medicines.json` `whereContentLives`), because the engine runtime's store has
none of the record's grant, purpose check or access log. Nothing here is a real service: no prescription reaches a
pharmacy, no bag is sealed, no PIN opens anything and no laboratory is contracted.

The Trust engine binds its routes on `packages/engines` from a new contract, `packages/catalog/verify-in-service.json`,
with its screens on the web, iOS and Android. Nothing here is a real service: no face is matched, no code reaches a
door, no desk is told and no complaint is reviewed by anybody.
