| Badge and gates | `GET /v1/trust/parties/{partyId}/badge@1` answers a tier id and `verified`, and nothing it was worked out from; somebody lapsed and somebody unknown get the same refusal. The reviewer's gates are worked out on read from the register.                                                                                                                           | Engine runtime                                             |
| Shift start     | The face-match door (`face-match-result` in `feeds.json`) answers not-integrated and refuses every payload; the shift is recorded as not matched, publishes `trust.shift_start.unmatched@1`, and the dispatch rule in force decides whether she is offered work. The desk's board says no face match was performed.                                                 | Engine; web Control Tower; iOS and Android nurse workspace |
| Door check      | The nurse shows six digits for the visit; the engine keeps an HMAC, never the digits. The patient types them and sees a name, initials where a photograph would be, and a badge tier. A wrong code says the tries left; the last one, or "this is not my nurse" at any time, publishes `trust.door.mismatched@1` and reports through `POST /v1/safety/incidents@3`. | Engine; web, iOS and Android                               |
| Complaints      | A patient complains about a past visit; it is reviewed within the window in force when it arrived. The reviewer's queue ages each against its own window, carries no account or complainant in a row, and decides nothing without a reason. The nurse's notices say a complaint exists, its kind and its outcome.                                                   | Engine; web patient and back-office Vetting tab            |
| Settings        | What an unmatched shift start does to dispatch (withhold), how long a door code lasts (5 min), how many tries (3), the complaint window (24 h, cited from the Full Scope's Engine 6), and who changes them — all proposals, on the Configuration tab.                                                                                                               | Engine; web back office                                    |

**What it refuses.**

- A Trust Score leaving Verify: the badge and door-check routes declare no number but the tries left, and no door-check screen names a score.
- Anything of a face: no Verify route, event, column or log carries a template, photograph, embedding or capture; a shift start takes no field and refuses one sent unread.
- "Matched" while no provider is integrated: no copy, screen or code says it, and nothing publishes `trust.shift_start.matched@1`.
- A complaint moving a score: no complaint code names a score, weight, tier or suspension; a decision publishes and calls nothing; a complaint or a decision carrying anything beside what the route declares is refused unread.
- A protected category in a complaint's header, and the complainant's identity reaching the nurse.
- Anybody acting on their own file: a shift, a code and a notice are the caller's own; a complaint about yourself, and a reviewer reading or deciding one about themselves, are refused.
- A typed code lifetime, try or review window on any platform.

**Withdrawn and versioned.** `POST /v1/trust/shift-starts@1` (carried a liveness capture reference and answered a match),
`POST /v1/trust/door-verifications@1` (a wrong code was a refusal, so no mismatch could leave the engine) and
`POST /v1/trust/complaints@1` (published `person.under_review@1` to dispatch engines on arrival) are withdrawn for
version two. `GET /v1/trust/parties/{partyId}/gates@1` is narrowed to the reviewer. `POST /v1/safety/incidents@3` adds
`engine:trust` and stays proposed. At the Wave 4 integration `POST /v1/trust/setting-changes@1` was withdrawn for
version two: it named the inside of `windows` and `parts` as `settings.json#types`, and Medicines & Labs had made the
shared change shape declare them, so version two is that shape with the same callers, refusals and handler.

## Delivered — Health Passport P1: share links, the export, the emergency card and the access log on the phones, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 4, Record P1)

A share link is a consent grant the patient already made, made narrower: the recipient and purpose are the grant's,
the scope sits inside it, and it ends when the grant ends, when the Record setting in force says, or at the founder's
ninety-day grant ceiling — whichever is first. Opening one is a read through the consent gateway, counted against its
uses and written into the patient's hash-chained log. The Passport P0 answers all of it, in development, on synthetic
data, on loopback, and the web preview asks the same arithmetic in `packages/engines/src/record/domain/links.ts`.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Where                                                                                                                                                                           |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`POST /share/link@2`, built.** Rides on a grant by `grantId`; `kindCode` is `share-link` or `emergency-card`; answers a secret shown once (`secretResponseFields`), kept only as a digest; emits `passport.share.link_created@1`. Version one is withdrawn: it named no grant, carried no secret and refused almost nothing, and listed a responder with no trip to check                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | A link to a scheme, an insurer or an employer (asked before any grant is looked up); a recipient who is not the grant's; a scope wider than the grant, or a sealed tick the grant did not give; sealed content without the patient's named tick, and a tick naming nothing; an emergency card beyond the emergency summary; a responder's link that is not a card; an end past the grant ceiling, past the setting in force, past the grant, or already past; a revoked or ended grant; an unknown kind — each in `passport-gateway.json`'s sentence, which the route declares word for word | `apps/passport/src/gateway.ts`, `packages/engines/src/record/domain/links.ts`, `packages/catalog/apis/record.json`, `passport-gateway.json`                                     |
| **`POST /v1/record/share-link-opens@1` and `share-link-revocations@1`, built.** The secret travels as `Link <secret>` on the Authorization header, never in an address; an idempotency key makes a retry the same use; emits `passport.share.link_used@1`. A new enforcement mechanism, `passport-share-link`, whose callers are the identifiable grant roles                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | A secret never issued or altered (one sentence for both); a revoked or ended link; a revoked or ended grant; a use beyond the uses; a use without a key. Revocation is one tap and idempotent                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `apps/passport/src/server.ts`, `packages/catalog/apis.json#enforcementMechanisms`                                                                                               |
| **`POST /export@2`, built.** The patient's own record as a FHIR R4 Bundle of synthetic data, with Provenance, the six exclusions and their sentences, and the plain statement that the Passport has no second factor so the export is not stepped up. Version one is withdrawn: it required a code the Passport cannot check and offered formats nobody built                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | An IPS document or a PDF; a sealed tick naming a category that is not sealed; sealed entries left out unless ticked by name; private entries, break-glass notes, the access log, keys and tags, grants and links always left out                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `apps/passport/src/gateway.ts`, `packages/catalog/passport-sharing.json#export`                                                                                                 |
| **Five Record settings**, on the Configuration screen and the engine runtime (`GET /v1/record/settings@1`, `POST /v1/record/setting-changes@1`): share link lifetime 7 days (1–90), share link uses 5 (1–20), default scope `emergency-summary` (or `whole-grant`), emergency card lifetime 30 days (1–90), card uses 10 (3–50). Every default and lowest bound is the Record P1 lead's proposal; each lifetime's highest bound is the founder's grant ceiling, held equal to `consent.json` by the build                                                                                                                                                                                          | A link keeps the end and uses it was made with; a change never lengthens one already made. The Passport P0 reads the defaults: the authenticated call from it to the engine runtime does not exist, so an admin's change reaches the web preview's next link and not the P0 service                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `packages/catalog/passport-sharing.json#settings`, `packages/engines/src/record/`                                                                                               |
| **The emergency card on three platforms.** A share link restricted to the emergency summary, shown or printed, saying first that it is a preview and not connected to any responder. The QR code reads as a sentence saying so, never an address. **iOS** draws it with CoreImage's `CIQRCodeGenerator`; **the web and Android** with an encoder written here (`apps/web/src/lib/qr.ts`, ported to `model/QrCode.kt`), because no QR library has been reviewed under `open-source.json` and §15D. Seven codes it drew were read back exactly by the macOS Vision framework and are kept in `tests/fixtures/qr-vectors.txt`; the build holds the web encoder to them and a JVM test holds Android's | A card that opens anything but the emergency summary, or anything sealed; a QR library in any manifest; a screen that types how long a card lasts or how often it opens                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `apps/web/src/features/PassportSharing.tsx`, `apps/ios/MyThuso/Features/PassportSharingView.swift`, `apps/android/app/src/main/java/za/co/mythuso/ui/PassportSharingScreens.kt` |
| **Who opened your record, on the web, iOS and Android.** Every access, allowed or refused, including link uses and break-glass with its reason sentence and review time, from a synthetic log generated into both phones; on the web, the patient's own link acts join it in the tab's memory                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Every reason is a gateway statement, refusal or break-glass reason named by id and resolved to its sentence; no role or action is shown without a label                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `packages/catalog/passport-sharing.json#preview`, `scripts/emit-passport-sharing.mjs`                                                                                           |

**What stays open.** `GET /v1/record/encounter-statuses/{encounterRef}@1` stays proposed: the Passport P0 stores no
Encounter resource, has no signature and no supersede, so there is no status to give. The frozen
`passport.share.link_created@1` and `link_used@1` list Core and Movement as subscribers and neither binds a handler
for them today; they gained `neverCarries` entries for the secret, clinical content and sealed categories, which do
not change their fingerprints, and no new version was declared because no engine acts on one. The shared settings
change shape leaves `windows` and `parts` in prose, so the inner-shapes rule carries a narrow exemption for a route
that is exactly that shape — a platform decision for the Platform Settings lead and the integrator. The web and
Android card are screens only in the preview: no link is stored anywhere but the tab or the screen, and nothing
scans to anything. A use without an idempotency key is refused as the shared `idempotency-key-required`, which
`share-link-opens@1` answers without declaring, as every route answers the shared refusals; the Passport answers it in
its own sentence rather than the shared "A money or dispatch write needs an idempotency key", as P0's
`purpose-not-allowed` already did. Whether the Passport keeps its own words for a shared id, or declares its own id in a
new version of the route, is for the integrator. The build now holds every P1 gateway method to refusing only what its
route declares or a shared refusal names, and resolves imports rather than matching their text when it keeps the P1
screens off the patient's first load.

## Delivered — Thuso Kit's registry, readings, wearable links and kits, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 4, Devices)

Devices is built on the engine runtime. Its contract is `packages/catalog/devices.json`; its routes are
`packages/catalog/apis/devices.json`, where every version-one route is withdrawn for a version that refuses what the
first could not; its arithmetic is `packages/engines/src/devices/domain`. **No reading's value is ever in Devices**:
the capturer asks Devices by everything but the value, every refusal is decided before anything is written, then the
capturer writes the Observation through the Passport's consent gateway under their own grant and names it. Devices
keeps references and states, because no route lets an engine write an Observation and the runtime's store has none
of the record's controls.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Where                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The device registry**: a device by serial with its class (certified, consumer, simulator), firmware, instrument kind and calibration; health worked out when read (reporting, stale, recalled, never synced; calibration in date, due, overdue, not tracked; battery; firmware; the settings version); a recall from a moment already passed                                                                                                                                  | duplicate-serial, device-class-not-declared, instrument-kind-not-in-the-kit, calibration-in-the-future; recall-without-reason, recall-reason-not-declared, recall-effective-in-the-future, already-recalled; device-not-registered. A recall marks every reading taken at or after its effective point — a row added beside the reading, never removed, the reading and its Observation untouched — and deletes none                    | `POST /v1/devices/registry@2`, `…/recall@2`, `GET …/health@2`; `devices.json` `marksMean`                                                                                             |
| **Readings in two steps**: asked for with the §5.5 schema minus the value (subject, device, metric, unit, taken at, source, quality, consent state, intended use, simulated), then linked to the Observation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | reading-without-source-and-quality, ingestion-after-withdrawal, simulator-as-real, consumer-device-clinical-weight (engine refusals, answered only by these routes); consent-state-not-declared, intended-use-not-declared, metric-not-declared, unit-not-the-metrics-unit (the unit is `records.json`'s), source-not-this-devices, recalled-device-clinical-weight; reading-not-waiting, observation-already-linked, linked-by-another | `POST /v1/devices/readings@2`, `POST /v1/devices/readings/{readingRef}/observation@1`                                                                                                 |
| **A consumer device never carries clinical weight, and never triggers dispatch or an alert — as arithmetic.** `carriesWeight()` asks five things of the contract: the class carries weight, the source is not simulated, the quality carries weight, no recall mark, meant for the record. `reading.ingested@1` is published only when it answers yes; `device.stale@1` only for a certified device; the engine calls no other engine. Both phones ask the same generated flags | A consumer reading meant for the record is refused; one meant as guidance is kept, marked and published on nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `devices.json` `clinicalWeight`, `domain/readings.ts`, `engine.ts`, `Models/Devices.swift`, `model/Devices.kt`                                                                        |

| **Kits** issued against the deposit in force, returned, lost, each in an append-only audit                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | kit-without-devices, kit-already-out, recalled-device-not-issued, device-already-in-a-kit; kit-not-issued, kit-already-closed; loss-without-reason, loss-reason-not-declared, loss-reported-by-another-nurse. **A loss charges nobody and no deposit is taken**: Money is Wave 5's                                                                                                                                                      | `POST /v1/devices/kits@2`, `…/return@2`, `…/loss@2`                                                                                                                                   |
| **Three proposed admin settings**: stale after (1 day, 1 hour–1 week), calibration shown as due (30 days, 7–90), kit deposit (R500, R100–R4,000), each changed by the admin on the Configuration tab                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | The shared settings refusals. A change never marks, unmarks or moves a reading; a kit keeps the deposit and version it was issued under. The stale interval is in the clinical review pack                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `devices.json` `settings`, `GET /v1/devices/settings@1`, `POST /v1/devices/setting-changes@1`, `lib/settings.ts devicesSettingsNow()`                                                 |

Each proven to fire by breaking its source                          | the Devices block at the end of `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`; `packages/engines/src/devices/*.test.ts`; `tests/devices.spec.ts`                                                     |

Still open. The three events were already frozen in Wave 2 and are published at version one; their declared
subscribers (Safety, Clinical, Core, Care, Money) bind no handler for a device event today, and removing them would
break the Wave 2 routes whose engine callers they justify — `POST /v1/safety/sentinel-deviations@1`,
`POST /v1/core/events@1` and `POST /v1/core/alerts@2`. `device.recalled@1` carries no effective point; a reader that
needs one reads the registry, or a version two is declared when that reader exists. The shared settings change shape
now declares the inside of `windows` and `parts`, because an engine adding its first settings after the prose-only
list was sealed could not declare a change route at all. Medicines & Labs declared it the same day with `parts` taken
from `settings.json#partTypes`; at the Wave 4 integration that declaration was kept, so `POST
/v1/devices/setting-changes@1`, frozen with `parts` from `settings.json#types`, was withdrawn for version two in the
kept shape with the same callers, refusals and handler, and the build's exception for change routes frozen in prose
was deleted because no live change route is on that list.

## Delivered — MyThuso for Mom Essential, vouchers and USSD booking, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 4, Gilbert & Access)

The monthly tier of MyThuso for Mom walked end to end as a preview, a voucher that pays towards a visit or a plan, and
a USSD menu that books a visit through the same rules as the app. Money holds the plan and the voucher; Access holds the
menu and the parent's summary grant; Medicines' existing chain carries the medicine. Nothing here is a real service: no
plan is sold, no debit order runs, no voucher is sold, no USSD code exists and no nurse is sent.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                                                                                                                |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Mom Essential**: a sponsor asks (`POST /v1/money/plan-subscriptions@1`), the parent agrees as herself and chooses how a sponsor's line reads (`…/accept@1`), and the first month's payable opens only then; paid through Money's simulated provider or a plan voucher, it starts the plan month. Money counts the month's included visit when it hears `appointment.booked@2` (a plan payable that owes nothing, paid to the nurse as a plan visit) and the month's collection when it hears `delivery.handed_over@1`. A plan read (`GET …/{subscriptionRef}@1`) answers the parent everything and a sponsor the day, with the kind of care and the service only if she chose service-named | `plan-not-offered` (Plus and Premium name things that do not exist), `plan-already-held`, `no-plan-buys-a-place-ahead` (mom-plans.json's sentence), `sponsor-agrees-for-the-parent`, `only-the-parent-agrees`, `line-detail-not-offered`, `subscription-not-found` | `mom-essential.json`, `money/domain/subscriptions.ts`, `money/domain/ledger.ts`                                      |
| **Visit summaries only under her grant**: the caregiver role, narrowed to `home-visit`, off until she turns it on, never longer than the role's ceiling; Money's plan carries nothing clinical either way                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | A grant made by anybody but the parent; longer than allowed; ended or stopped                                                                                                                                                                                      | `access/domain/sharing.ts`                                                                                           |
| **Vouchers**: issued by a corner shop or the back office at the catalogue's price (`POST /v1/money/vouchers@2`, version one withdrawn: it redeemed into a wallet that is not offered), redeemed in part or whole against a payable (`POST /v1/money/voucher-redemptions@1`); what is still owed is paid through `payments@2`; a cancelled visit's redemption goes back onto the voucher. The code is random, shown once and kept as a salted digest                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `voucher-cashes-out`, `voucher-tied-to-a-medicine` (section 18A), `voucher-towards-not-sold`, `voucher-not-found`, `voucher-expired`, `voucher-over-redeemed`, `voucher-more-than-owed`, `voucher-not-for-this`, `voucher-spent`                                   | `vouchers.json`, `money/domain/vouchers.ts`                                                                          |
| **Voucher expiry** as Money's setting `voucher-expiry-years`: 3 proposed, 3–5, the lowest bound cited from `vouchers.json` `expiry.law` — Consumer Protection Act section 63, marked as needing counsel's confirmation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | No voucher issued for fewer years than the law entry, whatever the setting says                                                                                                                                                                                    | `money.json`, `vouchers.ts` `expiryOn`                                                                               |
| **USSD booking**: the menu, its words and its refusals as data; a walk that books through `booking.ts` for whoever is nearest and times out by Access's setting `ussd-session-timeout-seconds` (120 proposed, 60–180); the `ussd-session` door in `feeds.json`, refusing every payload, with the `ussd-booking` capability. Everything on the menu is English in every locale (`locales.json` `clinicalRule`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `no-identity-number`, `no-card-details`, `no-clinical-detail`, `not-a-choice`, each said and the reply dropped; the door never accepts a reason for a visit, an identity number, a card number, a session's history or a cell location                             | `ussd.json`, `access/domain/ussd.ts`, `feeds.json`                                                                   |
| **Screens**: the Essential journey under the Mom plan on the web (its own dynamic import) and on iOS and Android from generated `MomEssentialData`, where no payment is taken and the plan never starts; a voucher at the booking's review step on the web; the USSD simulator on Language & access on the web                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | A sponsor is shown no control for agreeing; a phone says nothing was charged                                                                                                                                                                                       | `MomEssential.tsx`, `VoucherAtCheckout.tsx`, `UssdSimulator.tsx`, `MomEssentialView.swift`, `MomEssentialScreens.kt` |
| **The build** holds eight things: no plan ranks anybody, a plan screen lists only the tier's inclusions, nothing clinical reaches a sponsor without her grant, only the parent agrees, a voucher is never cash or tied to a medicine, the expiry bound is the law entry, no USSD screen passes 182 characters or asks for anything a session must not carry, and no new screen types a price                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Each proved by breaking its source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`                                                                                       |

No event was declared. Money is the only engine that acts on a plan or a redemption, and it hears what it needs on
events already frozen; an event nobody but its publisher acts on is refused by the event contract.

Still open: Plus and Premium are not offered; a plan stops only by the parent not agreeing, and stopping a started plan
is not built; only the first month is simulated; a delegate cannot authorise a medicine collector for a parent, because
`collection-authorisations@1` takes the patient alone; a sponsor with a grant is told summaries are shared and reads
none on these screens, because the Passport gateway is not reachable from them; gifts (`gifts@1`) stay proposed, because
a gift books for its receiver and a voucher does not, and no voucher route carries a beneficiary; vouchers and the USSD
menu are web only; the network's own USSD session limit is unknown until an aggregator states it; what happens to value
left on an expired voucher, and whether section 63 reaches a voucher towards care, wait on counsel.

## Delivered — the HL7 v2 bridge into the Health Passport, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 5, Trust & Record)

§26's `POST /hl7v2/inbound`, built in the Passport P0 and nowhere else: development only, synthetic messages only, from
one registered synthetic hospital and one registered synthetic laboratory, on the loopback. The contract is
`packages/catalog/hl7v2-inbound.json`; the reading and every rule are `packages/engines/src/record/domain/hl7.ts`, which
the Passport and the web preview both ask; every refusal sentence is `passport-gateway.json`'s. **MLLP is not served** —
no partner is connected, nothing checks a partner's certificate, and the Passport may have no network exposure — and a
partner's system still reaches the path only through the `hl7v2-inbound` door with mutual TLS, which is still to add.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`POST /hl7v2/inbound@1`, built, development only.** A developer holding a console-minted credential sends a registered partner's ER7 message as JSON; which partner it is comes from MSH-3 and MSH-4, never the credential. Every answer — accepted or refused — carries an HL7 ACK: AA, AE or AR in MSA-1 and the contract sentence in MSA-3. A retry under the same control ID with the same content is answered with its first ACK, word for word, and nothing is written twice. A **hand-written parser** reads MSH, PID-3, PV1-2/19/44/45, OBR-2/4/7/22/25 and OBX-2/3/5/6/7/8/11/16, and never NTE, NK1, IN1, DG1, AL1 or PD1, because no HL7 library has been reviewed under `open-source.json` and §15D                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | thirteen, each with its acknowledgement code: no developer credential; unreadable; a production processing ID; an unregistered sender; a kind this facility may not send; a control ID reused for a different message; a laboratory nobody contracted (Medicines' `labs.collectionModes`); a clock outside the skew in force, or without an offset; **no patient matched** (quarantined); a PID field with no consent basis; a lab order nobody placed for this patient; a result nobody verified (the lab-result door's own condition); an identity-shaped value                                                                                                              | `apps/passport/src/gateway.ts`, `server.ts`, `store.ts`, `packages/engines/src/record/domain/hl7.ts`, `packages/catalog/apis/record.json`                                                                   |
| **Matching on a number the patient linked, and on nothing else.** `POST /v1/record/patient-identifiers@1` (patient session) links a hospital number under a registered assigning authority, kept only as a keyed digest under a new partner key; a message is matched on its PID-3 identifiers alone — one patient or nobody — and **never on a name or a date of birth**, which the Passport does not hold. A matched message that carries any other PID field is refused in the patient's own chain and stores nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | identifier-authority-not-registered, identifier-is-an-identity-number (thirteen digits, whatever the authority), identifier-not-for-this-authority, identifier-already-linked — and the patient the number belongs to sees `identifier.link.contested` in their log                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `hl7v2-inbound.json` `matching`, `identifierLinks`, `pid`                                                                                                                                                   |
| **ADT A01 and A03 become one FHIR Encounter.** An admission writes it in progress; its discharge revises it to finished as version two, and version one is kept sealed in `resource_versions`; a discharge with no admission held writes it finished. The visit number is kept only as a tag, and a visit number already held for another patient is not matched. **What stays open.** `GET /v1/record/encounter-statuses/{encounterRef}@1` **stays proposed**: the Passport now stores an
Encounter, but a hospital's admission written by a partner's message, signed by nobody; it still stores no signature and
no supersede, so the route could only ever answer "written", and Care would wait for a signature that cannot arrive or
read an admission as the encounter a nurse completes a visit against. The three cross-service calls do not exist: the
Passport P0 is handed the lab orders Record heard placed and knows none in its own process, its hand-off to Medicines is
kept rather than sent, and it reads the Record settings' defaults — the web preview and the tests join the halves. A
clinician reads an HL7 result by the reference Medicines holds, under a grant; `GET /fhir/DiagnosticReport` and
`/fhir/Encounter` searches are not routed, and the export and a link leave both types out until new versions declare
them. The OBX-to-FHIR mapping has no clinical reviewer yet and is not in the review pack, which lists the discharge
follow-up proposals. ADT A08 and ORM are not built. No HL7 library is in the open-source register as a candidate, because
none was read from its primary source in this wave. Neither phone has a lab results screen.

## Delivered — Clinical Intelligence: the inbox and its signature, the consultation frame, and triage, guidance and outcome questions that wait on the board, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 5, Clinical Safety)

Clinical Intelligence is built on the engine runtime with **no clinical content of any kind**. Its contract is
`packages/catalog/clinical.json`: frames, empty registries, words and two settings. Its routes are
`packages/catalog/apis/clinical.json`, where the inbox, the signature, the consultation, triage and outcome questions
are withdrawn for versions that refuse what the first could not; its arithmetic is `packages/engines/src/clinical/domain`,
which the engine binds and the web preview runs. Clinical keeps references and states: a consultation's words, a
triage's reason codes, a script and a patient's answers are the Health Passport's.

| **The clinical inbox**: the visits Care hands over (`visit.handover.submitted@1`), each by reference, with the protocol version the visit named, whether the register holds it as ratified and whether its record is complete; read only by a confirmer the settings name whom Trust has cleared today                                                                | not-a-confirmer, state-not-declared                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `GET /v1/clinical/reviews@2`                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **A review signed by the confirmer's own action**: under the ratified protocol the visit named, or — for a draft or none — only as _reviewed outside any protocol_, which the signature says in the contract's sentence; signing starts the episode whose outcome questions keep the days in force                                                                    | auto-signed-note (engine), not-a-confirmer, no-such-review, review-already-signed, own-record, record-incomplete, signing-mode-not-declared, protocol-not-ratified, protocol-not-the-visits, sign-under-the-ratified-protocol. `review.signed@1` names a protocol version on its envelope only when it is ratified                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `POST /v1/clinical/reviews/{reviewRef}/sign@2`                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **The structured consultation**: the Passport entry and which SOAP headings it holds; a heading is required when a section it covers is required in `records.json`                                                                                                                                                                                                    | required-sections-missing, section-not-in-the-frame, consultation-already-signed-off, auto-signed-note (engine)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `POST /v1/clinical/consultations@2`                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **Triage as a frame**: GilbertOne's emergency terms first and untouched, the red-flag gate, a priority only with its reason codes, a model that may explain and never lower. No triage protocol is ratified, so every call is answered **not triaged**, with who decides                                                                                              | triage-without-ratified-protocol and model-lowers-priority (engine, answered by this route), priority-without-reason-codes, protocol-content-not-in-this-build                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `POST /v1/clinical/triage@3`                                                                                                                                                                                       |

Who confirms is read by the inbox and the signature whenever they are asked; the days are kept by the episode                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `clinical.json` `settings`, `GET /v1/clinical/settings@1`, `POST …/setting-changes@1`, `POST …/setting-reviews@1`, `lib/settings.ts clinicalSettingsNow()`                                                         |
| **Screens**: the doctor's inbox and signature on web, iOS and Android; the consultation frame on the web; the not-triaged and no-ratified-script answers in the nurse's Assessments and the doctor's Protocols on the web and in the phone's inbox; outcome questions on the patient's record on the web. All in the clinical workspace, off the patient's first load | Sentences word for word from the contracts; roles and days from the settings in force (web) or the generated defaults (phones)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `features/ClinicalIntelligence.tsx`, `lib/clinical.ts`, `Features/ClinicalInboxView.swift`, `ui/ClinicalInboxScreens.kt`, `scripts/emit-clinical.mjs`                                                              |

Still open. `review.signed@1`, `triage.completed@2` and `guidance.delivered@1` were frozen in Wave 2; their declared
subscribers bind no handler yet, and Money will subscribe to `review.signed` when claims act on it. Care does not yet
put the protocol a visit was carried out under on the handover's envelope, so every review from the engine names none.
The runtime holds a top-level response field to `required` without reading `nullable`, so the sign and consultation
routes leave an absent value out rather than answering null.

**Who confirms a clinical review is the review-confirmer setting, everywhere (16 September).** Every engine whose
settings wait on a clinical review — Access, Care, Medicines and Clinical — withdrew `GET …/settings@1` and
`POST …/setting-reviews@1`, which admitted a doctor from the vetting register's grant of `sign-clinical-review`, for
version two, which admits every clinical role the setting may name (doctor, nurse). The handlers confirm, and let read,
only the roles it names in force: `packages/engines/src/settings/shape.ts reviewersOf()` is the one answer, Clinical
reads it from its own store, and the other three ask the new `GET /v1/clinical/review-confirmers@1`, confirming nobody
if Clinical does not answer. The web's setting reviews and the doctor-review screen on web, iOS and Android ask the same
setting. Refusal: confirmers-read-by-engines. Build guarantee 11 in the Clinical Intelligence block holds it, and
`packages/engines/src/review-confirmer-reaches-every-engine.test.ts` proves a nurse refused and then admitted on Clinical
and on Care. Still open: a consultation's diagnosis field and a teleconsultation read `sign-clinical-review` as a
capability to diagnose and to consult, which is not confirming a review, and are unchanged.

## Delivered — Sentinel tiers one to three and safeguarding reports, 15 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 5, Safety)

Sentinel and safeguarding are built on the Safety engine. The contract is `packages/catalog/sentinel.json`; the routes
are in `packages/catalog/apis/safety.json`, where each Wave 2 proposal is withdrawn for a version that refuses what it
could not; the arithmetic is `packages/engines/src/safety/domain/sentinel.ts`. **No threshold, baseline rule or deviation
definition is anywhere in this build**: those come only from the clinical governance board, none is ratified, and every
evaluation answers _not evaluated_ with the reason as a sentence. **No value is ever in Sentinel**: a reading is kept by
the record entry its value was written to, a baseline by the readings it counts, and a tier by who raised it.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **A tier registry, one to three**: tier 1 is recorded and tells nobody, tier 2 is the nurse's own queue, tier 3 opens a Core concern owned by a doctor with a nurse behind them. The words are proposals. **Tier four is refused**: an unresponsive patient and a dispatch nobody pressed for need a certified tier-four device and ratified rules, and neither exists                                                                                                                        | tier-four-not-in-this-build, rung-not-on-the-sentinel-ladder                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `sentinel.json` `rungs`, `tierFour`; `closed-loop.json` `sentinel`                                                                                                                                     |
| **Baselines made only of readings with clinical weight — as arithmetic.** Devices publishes `reading.ingested@1` only when `carriesWeight()` says yes, and Safety admits a reading only from that event: a consumer or wearable reading, a simulated one, a poor sample and one taken as guidance never reach a baseline, and a tier raised on one is refused. A baseline keeps the window and minimum in force when it opened                                                                | no-clinical-weight-behind-it, not-this-patients-reading, reading-left-by-recall; nothing-heard-for-that-patient, sentinel-read-takes-no-filter                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `GET /v1/safety/sentinel-baselines@1`; the `reading.ingested@1` handler in `safety/engine.ts`                                                                                                          |
| **A tier raised by hand**, at the tier a named clinician chose, on a reading Sentinel heard; published as `sentinel.rung_raised@1`, from which Core opens one alert at the rung `closed-loop.json` names, with the ladder's time for the rung raised. Below it Core opens nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                             | deviation-carries-nothing-else, raised-by-nobody. Version one, which Devices called with a device class it set itself, is withdrawn with no callers                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `POST /v1/safety/sentinel-deviations@2`; `heardSentinelRung` in `core/engine.ts`                                                                                                                       |
| **Stale suspends, recalled removes.** Safety hears `device.stale@1` and suspends every baseline a reading from that device is counted in, until a newer reading from it arrives; suspended means shown as suspended and counting towards nothing, with nothing deleted. The stale interval is Devices' own setting, applied by Devices when it announces. Safety hears `device.recalled@1` and takes every reading from the device out of every baseline, kept by reference with when it left | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `device.stale@1`, `device.recalled@1` (Safety now a subscriber of both)                                                                                                                                |

Open for good, held for a safeguarding officer, and **not sent**: the report says a report to the South African Police Service or the Department of Social Development would be made by a person, that this one was not, and why. Core opens one concern for the desk, whose close never closes the report   | report-without-reporter, safeguarding-keeps-no-narrative, safeguarding-group-not-declared, safeguarding-category-not-declared; guardian-told-nothing-of-safeguarding, safeguarding-list-takes-no-filter. The kind of concern is never a header chip, never on the desk's list and never on the bus; the reporter is never in a response the reported party's side can read                                                                                                             | `POST /v1/safety/safeguarding-reports@2`, `GET /v1/safety/safeguarding-reports@1`, `safeguarding.reported@2` (version one, which carried the kind, withdrawn); `heardSafeguarding` in `core/engine.ts` |

| **Screens**: the patient's Sentinel state and a tier raised by hand for the nurse (under her kit) and the doctor (patient context); the safeguarding form for the nurse (assessments), the doctor and the desk; the desk's safeguarding list and the tier-three concern on the Control Tower's board. On iOS and Android the Sentinel state and the safeguarding form sit under the nurse's kit. Still open. **No safeguarding officer role is on the vetting register**: adding one needs its checks, its provenance in
`vetting-proposals.json` and a build block, and `vetting.json` is on the patient's first load, so a report is held open
for an officer nobody holds and no route closes one. **Statutory reporting is not integrated**, and which Acts may apply
is written as a proposal nobody with legal authority has reviewed. `device.recalled@1` still carries no effective point,
so Sentinel removes every reading from a recalled device rather than only the ones the registry marked. Care and
Clinical are no longer subscribers of `sentinel.rung_raised@1`, because neither binds a handler; an engine that comes to
act on it joins then. Core stays a declared subscriber of `reading.ingested@1` without a handler, because
`POST /v1/core/events@1` names that event as Devices' justification for calling Core. The Sentinel settings are changed
by whoever `settings-changed-by` names, which may include the Control Tower operator, although both wait on a clinical
review. On a phone a tier three is recorded and says who would be told; no phone has the Control Tower's board.

## Delivered — Thuso Ride and Access & Admission, 16 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 5, Movement)

Movement is built on the engine runtime. Its contract is `packages/catalog/movement.json`; its routes are
`packages/catalog/apis/movement.json`, where eleven of the twelve version-one routes are withdrawn for versions that
refuse what the first could not, `decline@1` is built as it was frozen, and three routes are new; its arithmetic is
`packages/engines/src/movement/domain`. **Thuso Ride is non-emergency transport and never an ambulance**, and
counsel's opinion on the emergency medical services boundary does not exist, so every refusal stays on the transport
side of a line nobody has drawn. **Movement holds references, codes, states and times**: why a patient travels or is
admitted is in the Health Passport.

A trip keeps its offers, window and retention; the interval is read when a heartbeat arrives                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | The shared settings refusals                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `movement.json` `settings`, `GET /v1/movement/settings@1`, `POST /v1/movement/setting-changes@1`, `lib/settings.ts movementSettingsNow()` |

| **Screens**: the nurse (Assessments) and the doctor (Patient context) request a trip, a P2 with its reason, and a P1 is refused with the emergency numbers above the refusal; the Control Tower's Dispatch carries every trip, the responder's phone as the desk sees it, admissions with pending shown as pending and the simulated facility answer, and the directory; the responder app on iOS and Android accepts, declines, reads the emergency summary only during the trip and hands over. Still open. **No responder role is on the vetting register**, so Verify can verify nobody to drive and every
heartbeat on the engine runtime is refused as not verified; the web preview treats its synthetic R-311 as verified
and says so. **No grant role exists for a receiving facility**, so the packet rides on the responder's trip grant and
the patient's access log names that role when a facility opens it. **The Passport P0 does not end a link at the
hand-over**: Movement ends the packet on its own side and marks an open after it, and ending the link at the Passport
needs an engine-callable revocation that does not exist. **A Thuso Ride capability of its own** was written and taken
out: the capability contract is projected whole onto the patient's first load, and one more capability cost 0.14 kB;
Thuso Ride answers under dispatch and emergency until the projection carries only what the first view reads.
admission.need_identified, facility.candidate_selected and the two pre-authorisation events are declared and not
published, because no triage decision, matching or scheme answer exists to publish them from. The Wave 2 subscribers of
the published events (Core, Access, Care, Record and Clinical) bind no Movement handler yet; the obvious one is Core
opening a concern for an admission that gets no answer. The offer lapse §12 describes (first to accept within 30
seconds) is not modelled: an offer stays open until it is declined or the trip is taken.

## Delivered — group payers and claims to a medical scheme, 16 September 2026 (seed plan, Wave 5, Money)

A stokvel, a church or an employer paying for the care of members who each agreed to it, and the billing half of a claim
to a medical scheme, drafted by the doctor who signed the visit's review and stopped before it is sent. **MyThuso holds
no group's money**, and **no claim reaches a scheme**: no switching partner is connected and no code set is adopted.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | The refusals it adds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Where                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Group payers**: a group is opened by the role its kind names (`POST /v1/money/groups@2`, version one withdrawn: it created a group wallet and named its members in the request), invites (`POST /v1/money/group-memberships@1`), the person invited agrees as herself and chooses how the group's screen reads what it paid for her (`…/accept@1`), may leave (`…/leave@1`), and asks the group to pay for a visit she owes (`POST /v1/money/group-payments@1`), which charges the group's own account through the provider up to the limit in force. The treasurer reads amounts and days (`GET /v1/money/groups/{groupRef}@1`); she reads her own memberships (`GET /v1/money/group-memberships@1`) | `group-holds-money`, `group-kind-not-offered`, `group-kind-not-yours`, `member-added-without-agreement`, `already-invited`, `group-full`, `only-the-member-agrees`, `employer-sees-health`, `group-line-detail-not-offered`, `not-a-group-member`, `group-pays-only-members`, `group-limit-reached`, `group-memberships-are-your-own` | `groups.json`, `money/domain/groups.ts`, `money/domain/ledger.ts`     |
| **No pooled money, and no wallet**: a group is a reference, a kind and who opened it, with no field that can hold an amount and no table named for a balance. `wallets@1` stays **proposed** and is never built — a prepaid balance MyThuso keeps for anybody is money held for other people, which only a bank may take (Banks Act 94 of 1990), and it waits on counsel and a licensed partner rather than on code                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | A request carrying a balance, a deposit, a pool, a wallet, a float or a contribution is refused rather than ignored                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `groups.json` `noPooledMoney`, `check-boundaries.mjs`                 |
| **An employer is told nothing about an employee's health**: no member rows at all, and the month's total and the number who agreed only above the employer programmes' suppression floor, read from `programmes.json` rather than restated. | **Claims**: drafted by the doctor who signed the visit's review (`POST /v1/money/claims@3`, version two withdrawn: it took a typed tariff code and an amount of the caller's and answered "submitted"), agreed to by the patient (`…/consent@1`), asked to be sent (`…/submit@1`) and read by either (`GET /v1/money/claims@1`). Money finds the signed review from `review.billable@1`, never from a `reviewRef` on a route — Clinical declares the `reviews` resource — and hears no `review.signed@1`, which carries the encounter reference                                                                                                                                                         | `claim-visit-not-finished`, `claim-review-not-signed`, `claim-review-not-yours`, `claim-code-typed`, `diagnosis-sent-to-money`, `claim-already-drafted`, `claim-without-consent`, `claim-consent-expired`, `claim-consented-already`, `scheme-told-about-a-grant`, `claims-are-your-own`                                              | `claims.json`, `money/domain/claims.ts`                               |

The `claim-response` door in `feeds.json` refuses every payload, names its operator and leaves section 72 undetermined, and the `scheme-claims` capability is absent. The code gate is asked after it, because a code set is adopted with the switching partner; nothing in the ledger can mark a claim sent, answered, paid or rejected                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `claim-not-submitted`, `claim-without-an-adopted-code`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `feeds.json`, `capabilities.json`, `ledger.ts` `submitClaim`          |
| **No code set**: no licensed tariff code set and no adopted ICD-10 code set exist, so a claim carries no code — none typed, none chosen from a list and none worked out from a service's name — and the doctor's draft leads with "No adopted code set"                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | A request carrying a field named for a code, a coding, a diagnosis or a reason is refused in the code-set sentence                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `claims.json` `codeSets`                                              |
