The app's own state machine did not change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `tests/assistant.spec.ts`                            |

**What did not move.** The demonstrator's widget keeps its own rig and its own greeting idiom — the same
development-only double-fire exists there and is unfixed, because that surface has its own tests and its own
reviewer. The cue manifest, its timings and its priority order are unchanged; nothing in `lib/gilbertone.ts`
moved. The two voice registers stay separate, `voice.webSentences` for a patient and `voice.webPoc.sentences`
for the demonstrator, and the microphone delivery above stands as written. iOS and Android still render the
assistant as the 17 September renaming left it; the character is web-only until the founder asks for it on
the phones. Draft PR #5 is superseded by this pass — it deleted the sphere without re-expressing the tests,
and deleting assertions to make a suite pass is the opposite of what the assertions are for.

## Delivered — the character's affect, decided 19 September 2026 (web only, phase 2 of one GilbertOne)

The founder's "one GilbertOne" decision said affect is deterministic and derived from the answer kind
alone — no model — and its second phase makes that a contract: a dated `affect` section beside the
matcher whose answers it maps, giving every answer kind a cue and a posture. The situations wear A13's
brief warm smile (warm), the identity and voice answers wear A17's flat face while their refusals are
stated (flat), the emergency holds A16's steady face (flat), unmatched and a turn with unread words
wear A12's lifted brow (concerned), and the handover holds A11's smaller smile (attentive). The
founder's own four words — routine to warm, refusal to flat, unmatched to concerned, emergency to
A16 — are pinned by the build, and the kinds are derived from the contract itself: a new answer kind
added to `questions` or `answers` without a face fails `npm run check` the day it is added.

The panel no longer chooses a face. `cueOf` reads the reply's kind from the contract, with the
unread override the section itself explains: a turn whose words could not be read wears the concerned
face whatever else it matched, because the unread block in that turn is a refusal. The posture is
read off the cue that owns the face, so the readable `data-affect` cannot say warm over a face A16 is
holding. And the invariant that outranks everything else — **affect may never soften a refusal or an
emergency** — is enforced by running the rig's real reducer inside `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`,
not by paraphrasing its rules: A16 takes the flat face from a held attentive pose and keeps it against
every other cue in the manifest, from later turns and from stale ones, on the animated path and under
reduced motion, through every step and through settling, and only Start again — the patient's own
reset — rests it.

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | What it refuses                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Where                                            |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| **The affect section as data** — every answer kind with a `cue`, a `posture` and a written `why`; the greeting idiom (`openFirst` once, `openAgain` on a re-open, the blink and idle drift named as additive channels that never own the face); and eight dormant cues in `notWired`, each with the reason it is not wired                                                                                             | The founder's four words pinned: routine warm, refusal flat, unmatched concerned, emergency the one cue on the safety track — held, flat, undisplaceable. The posture vocabulary is closed at four words and each is honest against the cue it names — warm needs a smile, flat a flat mouth, concerned a lifted brow, attentive a tilt — and never warm over the five refusal-bearing kinds | `packages/catalog/assistant.json` `affect`       |
| **The seam asks the contract.** `cueOf` maps the kind — unread overriding the match — and `postureOf` reads the posture off the running cue; the greeting effect plays the conversation cues; the panel types no cue id of its own                                                                                                                                                                                     | A boundary check fails on a typed cue id in the panel or the lib, on a greeting not read from `affect.conversation`, on a posture not read off the rig's running cue, and on the panel calling the rig's `stop` — the words and the face cannot drift apart because neither is written twice                                                                                                 | `lib/assistant.ts`, `Assistant.tsx`              |
| **The never-soften invariant, proven by the reducer itself.** The gate imports the rig over node's type-stripping and runs `reduce`: A16 holds against all sixteen other cues from later and stale turns on both motion paths, through advance and settle; an emergency takes the face from a held A11; only `rest` releases it. Sixteen ways of breaking the guarded sources were tried and every one fails the build | Every cue in the manifest is accounted for — wired, conversation, or `notWired` with a reason — so a cue cannot appear on the face that no contract sentence explains                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, the affect block |
| **The spec reads the contract, not the manifest.** `faceOf(kind)` holds each kind to its own cue and posture on both viewports — the greeting on open and re-open, the warm situation, the two flat refusals, the concerned unmatched and unread, the held emergency, the attentive handover, and Start again releasing cue and posture together                                                                       | Phase 1's typed A09/A11/A16 assertions are retired with the mapping, because the face is the contract's now; A13's smile is a gesture, so it is asserted at once before it settles; and under reduced motion the emergency still holds its flat face, because a still face can still be an urgent one. 46 journeys pass and 4 skip by design — a phone's arithmetic                          | `tests/assistant.spec.ts`                        |

**What did not move.** The rig itself: no cue, timing, track or priority order in `lib/gilbertone.ts`
changed — the file's import specifiers gained the extensions node's type-stripping needs, which is
what lets the gate run the real reducer instead of a copy of its rules. The three decisions the
founder closed stay closed: no model — the same answer wears the same face every time because the
face is data; roles demo-scoped and labelled simulated until the identity service exists; speech
built behind a contract flag and off, with A10 in `notWired` saying so. The phones are untouched —
`emit-assistant.mjs` deliberately emits none of this, because cue ids are the web rig's vocabulary
and iOS and Android keep the six states the 17 September renaming left them — and the demonstrator
keeps its own rig, its own greeting idiom and its own tests.

**The affect costs the patient's entry nothing, measured rather than presumed.** The entry was built
and measured with this change stashed and with it applied: 282.62 kB across the same 13 files, byte
for byte identical both ways, because the contract rides the assistant's lazy import and the entry's
asset list does not mention it. That the tree stands 0.46 kB over the 282.16 kB CLAUDE.md records
from `8bf3e48` is therefore not this change's doing — it came with the work that landed between 16
and 19 September (the live assistant's microphone, the engine bridge, the panel fixes, the rig
itself), and whether it is accepted or taken back is the founder's decision, not a line item to
absorb quietly.

## Delivered — the second tier's production gate, 21 September 2026 (bridge live, service dark)

The web panel's bridge to GilbertOne's second tier had shipped ahead of its paperwork: a service behind it with a wildcard CORS header, an unguarded paid endpoint, a consent line that still said no model ever receives what a person says, and a permissions header still refusing the microphone the live assistant was given on 19 September. This pass closes each of those where it was open, and makes the second tier something a deploy can deliver in full without ever switching it on. All four states it can be in — **bridge shipped**, **runtime installed but disabled**, **provider configured**, and **production operational** — are named apart in `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, `deploy/README.md` and `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md`, because each is a different answer to "is it working".

| What landed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | What it refuses, and what did not move                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Where                                                                                         |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The production acknowledgement gate.** `MYTHUSO_ASSISTANT_PRODUCTION=acknowledged`, written by hand into the service's own env file and by nothing else — not the unit, not the credential script, not a deploy. In production with a provider configured the service prints its refusal and exits before binding its port; `orchestrate()` returns the on-device answer without it even where a local model would otherwise be probed                                                                          | **A configured key can no longer switch the public model on by itself** — the operator's second, deliberate act is the gate. The refusal message names the variable and never a value, because the process may be holding the key. `/assistant/health` reports `production` and `activated` as booleans beside `azure` and `ollama`, and still nothing that could carry a secret       | `lib/activation.ts`, `server.ts`, `lib/orchestrator.ts`, the boundary block reading all three |
| **The browser origin policy.** The wildcard is gone: in production exactly `https://mythuso.co.za` and `https://www.mythuso.co.za` are answered, localhost shapes and a development-only variable in development, and any other `Origin` is a 403 before any route reads the URL. Allowed origins are echoed with `Vary: Origin`; a request with no `Origin` — curl, a checker — is answered as before                                                                                                            | **Every route here is unauthenticated, so a wildcard was any page on the internet using this deployment as a free proxy to the paid provider.** The development variable is never read in production, and the boundary check reads the policy file for the two names and the absence of `*` in either file                                                                             | `lib/origin-policy.ts`, boundary checks                                                       |
| **The endpoint's own fence in nginx.** The `/assistant/` location proxies `127.0.0.1:8791` same-origin with a 768 KB body limit matching the service's own constant, a per-address `limit_req` of 60 r/m with a burst of ten answering 429, forwarded headers, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, and the full security-header set redeclared inside the location                                                                                                                                                           | **The rate limit is scoped to `/assistant/` alone** — `limit_req_zone` is declared at the http level and `limit_req` appears only in this one location, held there by a boundary check, so the five co-tenant sites inherit nothing. HSTS stays `max-age=300`, the reload stays a reload, and the identity block stays commented out                                                   | `deploy/nginx/mythuso.conf`, boundary checks                                                  |
| **The microphone policy, corrected to match the shipped assistant.** Both nginx `Permissions-Policy` declarations — server level and `/assistant/` — now carry `microphone=(self)`, so the site's own pages may prompt while no other origin may reach the microphone through this host                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | **camera=() and geolocation=() are unchanged, and the identity service's own policy stays `microphone=()`** — its header is about a service with no microphone anywhere in its product, and a boundary check holds both of those facts, and holds the two nginx declarations identical                                                                                                 | `deploy/nginx/mythuso.conf`, `apps/api/src/server.ts`, boundary checks                        |
| **The runtime, delivered deterministically.** `npm run assistant-runtime` bundles the service into one self-contained `apps/assistant-api/dist/server.mjs` (esbuild, knowledge catalogs included, credential-shaped strings refused before publish); the deploy builds it before the first `ssh`, refuses a server whose Node is under the `engines` floor, publishes it atomically by sha256, and installs the unit **dark** — no macOS `node_modules` ever reaches the box, and no deploy enables the service   | **An assistant that will not build is a deploy that mutated nothing**, and a Node that is too old is a loud skip rather than a broken site. Until credentials exist the correct state is `assistant  not enabled`, reported as a fact rather than a failure, exactly as the identity line has always been                                                                              | `scripts/build-assistant.mjs`, `deploy/deploy.sh`, `deploy/ops/assistant-api.service`         |
| **The credential door.** `deploy/ops/configure-assistant-env.sh` — root-only, `umask 077`, the key read with the echo off, the endpoint validated as bare HTTPS, the file assembled with `mktemp` and moved over the old one only after every check, `0600 root:root`, shell variables unset, and a fingerprint printed instead of the key                                                                                                                                                                        | **The script never writes the acknowledgement** and the boundary check refuses it if it ever does — typing the key and taking the production decision are two separate acts. The RUNBOOK repeats the rule at the top: the key goes into that prompt and nowhere else, never a chat, a commit, a command line or a log                                                                  | `deploy/ops/configure-assistant-env.sh`, `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, boundary checks                 |
| **The truthful consent.** `packages/catalog/assistant.json` no longer says GilbertOne does not send what you say to MyThuso. It says: what you type — or what your browser or keyboard turns your speech into — may be sent to the language model provider configured for MyThuso after details that could identify you have been removed; no audio recording is kept; do not type identifying health information; and when the approved sentences can answer, no model is asked and the words stay on the device | **The emergency and refusal language did not move a word**, and nothing was softened: the same red flags, the same matcher, the same numbers. The web disclosure is web-only and the emitters confirmed no native file changes; the powered-by line no longer claims the assistant uses only approved sentences, because a refined answer is still possible when the second tier is on | `packages/catalog/assistant.json`, the assistant emitter run                                  |
| **The build holds all of it.** `npm run check` now ends in `node scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, so the documented command is the enforced one — and the boundary block reads the assistant's contract, its service files, the nginx location, the deploy's publish and build wiring, the credential script's six safe properties, the microphone pair, the acknowledgement's presence in the RUNBOOK and its absence from the unit and the script                                                                 | **Identity remains disabled, no capability became connected, and the checks refuse the drift rather than the wording**: they read the entry point the build names, the paths the unit runs, the origins the policy holds, and the booleans' absence from anywhere but the code that computes them                                                                                      | `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, `package.json`, `apps/assistant-api/src/deploy.test.ts`       |

**What did not move.** The fifteen capability declarations, untouched — `/status` still reports every one of them as not connected, because a conversational tier is not an integration, and nothing in this pass marks screening, identity, Passport, payments or anything else live. The on-device matcher and its emergency precedence. iOS and Android: no permission, no file and no behaviour. HSTS at 300 seconds, nginx reload-only, and the identity service dark. **What the second tier still needs before real patient text is acceptable is not code**: the Azure data-processing and residency decision, and the deployment approval the plan names — the acknowledgement line is where an operator confirms both were taken.

Phase 0 of the founder's Control Tower & GilbertOne Commercialization plan is the part that needs no person, no supplier and no credits: the contracts every later phase is built against, and the build checks that hold them. **No screen, no route handler and no runtime code was written.** Every contract below says of itself that nothing it describes runs.

| **Clinical configuration tiering** — Tier 1 inventoried by id (12 escalation rules, 10 emergency-term groups) and a Tier 2 per-tenant template | **No regex, word or message is copied out of Tier 1**; a Tier 2 rung is capped below emergency and evaluated after every Tier 1 rule. "Locked" is kept apart from "clinically reviewed", which the emergency terms still are not | `gilbert-clinical-core.json`, `tenant-clinical-layers/_template.json` |
| **Inference isolation and the knowledge-corpus split** — decisions 6.3 and 6.2 as contracts | **No tenant exists anywhere in the service yet**, so nothing is isolated; the contract names the shared session store and the shared 200-entry retrieval cache as what would have to change. | **The device lifecycle** — pairing paths, health indicators, thresholds and trust | **Every pairing transport requires physical-possession proof; `thresholds` is empty and every future row needs a ratified queue entry; a consumer device never creates an incident** — already true through `devices.json` and `reading.ingested@1`, now guarded. | **Compliance pack, offboarding and break-glass**, as schemas | **Each section is gated by the governance document that keeps it empty.** Offboarding records that the Passport has no deletion mechanism at all — a code gap, not a governance one. Break-glass grants end within four hours and notify a second person, and no notification mechanism exists to do it | `compliance-pack.json`, `offboarding.json`, `break-glass.json` |
| **Reconciliation documents** — the tab inventory, the session model, the regulatory-classification question, the threat model and adversary register | **The live role switcher grants no permission and is not default-deny**; the regulatory question is sharpened for a lawyer, not answered. `COMMERCIAL-AND-CONTROL-TOWER-SCOPE.md` now has fifteen admin layers and flags where the fuller device specification lives | `docs/control-tower-*.md`, `docs/governance/REGULATORY-CLASSIFICATION.md`, `docs/security/` |

**What did not move.** No bundle byte: nothing here reaches `apps/web`. | **Generated, never typed** — `scripts/emit-crisis-lines.mjs` writes `CrisisLinesData.swift` and `CrisisLinesData.kt`, reusing the `GilbertLine` type the ambulance numbers use | Swift escapes quotes; Kotlin escapes backslash, quote and `$` | `npm run crisis-lines`, the `generated` list |

A live action without all three of its record, or with a refusal beside it, fails the build; a third live action fails it by name | `packages/catalog/control-tower-portal.json#gilbertone` |
| **The price** — Azure Speech's card records `pricing`: USD 16 per million characters, the standard neural tier's list price copied by hand on 2026-09-27 with its source | **A list price, not a measurement**: the card says so, no bill has been read, and `voice.json#previewPanel.costAndLatency.value` stays null, and its `why` says the estimate is the card's list price times the sentence's characters, never a measurement | `packages/catalog/api-registry.json` |

## Delivered — the Control Tower in the pastel system: figure cards, rings, pill bars and controls that are the native ones, restyled, 27 September 2026 (web only)

The founder's brief of 27 September 2026: the Control Tower screens did not look designed — soft tinted cards with one large numeral each, rings and rounded bars that arrive once, pill tabs and controls, generous space, ink on tint and one dark card for emphasis. Done through the shared parts rather than screen by screen, so all fourteen categories and both roles (`/app/?role=control-tower`, `/app/?role=back-office`) inherit it.

| What landed | What it refuses, and what did not move | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Figure cards and the ring** — `Figures` (a chip, one numeral at 64/40px in a light weight, its name, an optional note) and `Ring` (an SVG share on `stroke-dashoffset`, rising once from empty under `[data-decor='on']`) in the shared parts; four figures on the Overview (open gates as the dark card, services with a ring of those connected, discrepancies, changes), four on Devices & Fleet (real devices allowlisted as the dark card, classes, indicators with a ring of those built today, thresholds), the Overview's sample week, and the Dispatch strip — the workspace's own metrics each set in a card, the lead figure dark and "Parties blocking work" ringed by its share of the register its chip names. Numerals line up across a strip by subgrid | **No number is typed**: every numeral counts a list the same screen shows or a contract holds, a ring is drawn only where the chip or the numeral already states both halves of the share, and the ring is `aria-hidden` because the words say it. **No tint by position**: a card's tint comes from its own name (`tintsFor`, collisions resolved alphabetically), the rotation by `nth-child` on the week's bars is gone, and a bar's colour is its half of the week — mint and peach, the ring's own two. **The tints are the tokens'**: mint, lavender, peach and pale green resolved on `:root` from `brandMint`, `studioLilac`, `studioPeach` and `studioLime`, above the clinical shell's remap, each carrying the ink its `tokens.json#contrast` row measures. **No tint carries a word**: every tinted rule sets its own ink | `features/portal/Parts.tsx`, `features/portal/Overview.tsx`, `features/portal/Devices.tsx`, `features/portal/Operations.tsx`, `features/portal/design-widgets.css`, `features/portal/portal.css` |

Phase 4's check 2 now admits an `<input type="radio">` in Voice.tsx and VoicePreview.tsx and nothing else (no text or password input, and no GilbertOne file may import the shared controls, so every input is written where the check reads it); check 4's preview half now requires the chips fieldset live under `previewMaySaveAsDefault`, a disabled fieldset with no handler otherwise, and every radio inside the two — each proved by breaking it and restoring byte for byte.
