Every tinted rule sets its ink in the same rule; a flagged reading keeps the charcoal chip, so the difference is never the tint. Because the build places this sheet first and the dev server does not, every rule wins on specificity, never on order | `features/Dashboard.tsx`, `surface/approved-care.css`, `surface/revamp.css` (home rules removed), `components/UI.tsx` (`tintOf`, additive) |
| **Sections arrive, and the tab pill slides.** Every direct child of `main` rises eight pixels and fades in over `--t-settle` when a section opens; the home's cards come in three steps over `--t-enter`, a third of `--t-quick` apart. On a phone one lime pill behind the five tabs moves to the chosen tab by transform, its index written by the shell as `--m-tab`. The hero and wellbeing buttons press (`.m-press`); cards keep patient.css's existing hover lift | **Gated on `[data-motion='on']`**, which `PatientShell` sets in a layout effect before the first paint and never under reduced motion — so nothing starts at opacity 0 for a reader who asked for stillness or a page whose script has not run. Each is removed with `!important` in `motion.css`'s reduced-motion block; only the token durations and `--ease-soft`; nothing endless; no router and no View Transitions API — the old panel is gone on the same frame, so there is no layout jump and nothing waits. The pill is a pseudo-element with no pointer events and hides when no tab is chosen; each tab keeps its name, `aria-current` and colour change. The two ungated `rise` rules on `.page-intro` in `patient.css` went into this | `surface/motion.css`, `shells/PatientShell.tsx`, `surface/patient.css` |
| **The catalogue priced in figures.** A live card wears its category's tint and its price is the figure (the word "From" small, the amount large and light — the text is still "From R 249"); a planned card stays untinted on paper, dashed. The categories are one segmented pill track. A ring beside "9 bookable now · 15 in the catalogue" draws that share and rises once — a conic sweep on one registered custom property (`--m-share`), finite, removed under reduced motion | **The ring states nothing the words do not** — both halves are in the sentence beside it, and it is `aria-hidden`. "Preview · No real visit is booked here" and the phase-one refusal are where they were, word for word. **Tried and reverted:** the catalogue behind its own dynamic import. It paid for this change with a kilobyte to spare, and `tests/journeys.spec.ts` refused it — the "Book a nurse" screen was empty on the frame it opened, which on a slow connection is the main booking section blank. The test is right; the bytes were found elsewhere | `features/Pages.tsx`, `surface/approved-care.css`, `surface/motion.css` |
| **Booking: the service on mint, a track that fills, the review on paper.** The service rides at the top of the dialog on mint with its price as the figure; the step dots are a full-width segmented track whose current segment fills once when the step arrives; the review's lines are one paper panel on a peach ground | On the booking's own chunk (`features/booking-look.css`), so none of it reaches a patient who never books. `StepHead` is untouched and its `role="img"` label is the same. The notices, the consent gate and the refusals are where they were | `features/Booking.tsx`, `features/booking-look.css` |
| **Passport and Live well.** The three reading charts are stat tiles on lavender with a paper reference lane and the chart's own "Within range" chip; a screen's own sections (the passport's, the visits') are one segmented pill track; the record's actions are soft paper rows two across at a desk with the icon on lime; the "Thuso Pass" chip is ink on lime (it was pale on pale). Live well's opening card is the dark card | **No sparkline invented and no grade composed**: the charts are the record's own series, as before. The sample-data notices stay first | `surface/patient.css`, `surface/revamp.css` |

**What paid for it.** Rules in the sheets this change owns that drew nothing: the ground's aurora `::after` in `patient.css` (hidden by `revamp.css` since the editorial pass, 111 bytes gzipped for nothing), the home's old figure-strip columns, rules for `.plan-card.featured`, `.ghost`, `.promo-card`, `.home-columns` and `.stated-label` (no source names them), and declarations `revamp.css` set and then overrode itself.

**What did not move.** The GilbertOne launcher and panel, the clinical workspaces, the portal, the landing page and every native app. No test was altered. **The patient's first load: 281.09 kB across 15 files**, measured the documented way in a clean worktree at `70ae8f78` with only this change applied, against **281.11 kB** at `70ae8f78` without it — the other sessions' uncommitted work kept out of both. **Not done:** brand ink on brand mint is measured in `tokens.json#contrast` only as the reversed pair (mint on ink, 8.09) and should be a row of its own; the production build places `revamp.css` before `patient.css` while the dev server places it after — worked around here by specificity, not fixed; the dead home rules in `app.css`, `core.css` and `patient-screens.css` (`home-hero`, `quick-tile`, `home-columns`, the onboarding panel and more) are outside this change's sheets and were left.

## Delivered — GilbertOne speech settings: ElevenLabs beside Azure, every knob an administrator's setting, the own voice the administrator's alone, 28 September 2026

The founder's ask, on the scope in `docs/scope/07-GilbertOne-Speech-Settings-2026-09-28.md`: ElevenLabs as another provider, and every Azure and ElevenLabs setting that is not a key, a region or a clinical register on the GilbertOne settings — with one amendment, that the own-voice feature is in scope and only an administrator changes it, which today is the founder alone.

| What landed | What it refuses, and what did not move | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The contract** — 22 settings in `voice.json`'s block (changelog version 2): the provider per presentation register, the fallback, two ceilings in characters, Azure's speed, pitch, encoding and profanity handling, the two call timeouts, ElevenLabs' model, stability, similarity, style, speaker boost, speed, encoding and latency mode, and `own-voice`; an `ownVoice` record (the founder's, dated, with the consent it stands as and the refusal of anybody else's voice); four locked items; six refusals. ElevenLabs' card `built · not-configured` with five environment lines (the key, the region, and the account's own identifiers per label and for the own voice), four hosts none in South Africa, the languages each model lists (v2 and Flash: English; v3: English and Afrikaans; none isiZulu, isiXhosa or Sesotho), the pay-as-you-go price recorded by hand, G33, and the founder's prohibition kept. `POST /v1/speak@4` adds the optional `register` and the `spoken-answer-ceiling-reached` refusal; version three is withdrawn with no callers, recorded as a narrowing | **Every default is a proposal** until the founder has listened, except own-voice, which the founder decided. Speed and pitch are counts of percent, because a percentage cannot pass a hundred. No setting names a clinical register; every one is the admin's and says what it never reaches. A voice identifier lives on the box and never in this repository or a response; a voice that is not the administrator's own is refused | `packages/catalog/voice.json`, `api-registry.json`, `apis/assistant.json` (+ the three locks), `control-tower-portal.json`, `assistant.json` |
| **One reader** — `speechSettingsOf()` and `tuningFor()` in the assistant engine's domain: the keys named once, and the one decision that a presentation register gets its provider and every knob while a clinical register, clinical assist or no register gets the platform default with the delivery mechanics alone and no own voice | Held by a test and a build check to that shape; the web preview and the service both ask it, so neither can apply a knob the other refuses | `packages/engines/src/assistant/domain/settings.ts` |
| **The service** — `providers/elevenlabs.ts` on the shared seam (the vendor's documented route, the key in its header, logging off on every request, the knobs as fractions for a presentation register only, the contract's voice name back and never an identifier); Azure's adapter applies speed and pitch as SSML prosody only away from a hundred, the encoding header, the recognition profanity parameter and the timeouts; `selectedSpeech()` decides the speaking card per request from the register, falls back to the environment's selection when the setting says so, and asks the monthly ceiling before any provider; the route refuses an unknown register and answers the ceiling at the contract's status | **Written from the published reference and not exercised against the live API** — the header and the card say so. In production an offshore card a setting names is refused in the registry's sentence and printed once at start-up; the default reads instead. **The service reads the contract's defaults**: it keeps no settings history, so a change on the Control Tower reaches the web preview and not the box, and the screen says so. The ceiling is counted in memory per calendar month and starts again on a restart, as the contract says. 33 new service tests; 369 pass | `apps/assistant-api/src/lib/{speech-settings,speech}.ts`, `providers/{seam,elevenlabs}.ts`, `server.ts`, their tests |
| **The Control Tower** — the eighth GilbertOne sub-screen, Speech settings, under G33: what is in force per register, each built provider's card with its residency, price and own settings, what is not a setting, the founder's own-voice record, and the Configuration tab's editor embedded fixed to the assistant engine; the Voice screen's preview reads through the provider in force for the chosen register, says that provider's cost, and refuses Play past the session ceiling; the patient panel and the preview send the register | The screen draws no field, control or handler of its own — every change is the shared editor's, with its rules and history. The emergency answer still asks for the platform's voice and now names its clinical register, which no setting reaches | `features/portal/gilbertone/{SpeechSettings,VoicePreview}.tsx`, `GilbertOne.tsx`, `Configuration.tsx` (`fixed`), `lib/{settings,gilbertone-admin,gilbertone-service,voice}.ts` |
| **Build checks** — the eighth sub-screen; the preview's POST with its register at the live version; the panel's speak call carrying the class; a native client held to live versions only; a settings choice may carry a vendor's spelling; a percentage may be nought where its setting says so; and the speech-settings block: one reader over every key, defaults held to the encoding and timeouts they restate, provider choices held to the built cards, no clinical register named, the domain's shape, the route's refusal, the own-voice record, guardrail and adapter, ElevenLabs on no phone and nowhere in `packages/gilbertone`, the key in both secret scans, the eighth screen embedding the editor and saying the service reads defaults | Fourteen breaks proved, each restored byte for byte; a fifteenth (a speech setting changed by a role other than admin) is refused first by the older settings-route callers check | `scripts/check-boundaries.mjs`, `settings-defaults.mjs`, `emit-assistant.mjs`, `build-assistant.mjs`, `deploy/deploy.sh` |
| **Ops and docs** — the credential script's ElevenLabs section (identifiers hidden, optional lines left out rather than written empty); the RUNBOOK's paragraph; the scope on file | The script still writes no selection line | `deploy/ops/configure-assistant-env.sh`, `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, `docs/scope/07-GilbertOne-Speech-Settings-2026-09-28.md` |
| **Journeys** — Speech settings on both viewports: the sentence that the service reads defaults, every register's provider and voice, every provider's card and settings, the locked items, the own-voice record, no key-shape, and the Azure speed changed to ninety through the embedded editor with a refusal for a missing reason, review, confirm and the card showing it; every preview reading and the patient's two readings name their register, the routine one a presentation class and the emergency one a clinical-delivery class; the Configuration journey reads a percentage | — | `tests/gilbertone-admin.spec.ts`, `tests/configuration.spec.ts`, `tests/safety-settings.ts` |

**What did not move.** The clinical-delivery pin, `escalation.ts`, the emergency terms, the on-device conversation mode and the phones' own synthesisers: ElevenLabs is named on none of them and the build fails if it is. Speech-to-text's provider stays a hand-written line. The 45-second cap, push-to-talk and captions stay founder decisions. No lock line was edited; speak@4's three lines and speak@3's narrowing were appended. The patient's first load: **274.28 kB gzipped at level 9 across 15 files** after `npm run build -w @mythuso/web`, measured the same way as before — on a tree that also carries other sessions' uncommitted web work, so it is not a figure for this change alone; what this change adds to the patient path is one field on a request.


## Delivered — the settings screens change-first, and controls that move, 28 September 2026 (web only)

The founder, of the Control Tower's Configuration tab and GilbertOne's Speech settings: put what an administrator can change at the top and the current state to the side, or folded on a phone, so the editor is the first thing seen; and give the forms the life of the Jitter UI-element templates — toggles that slide, buttons that glow, progress that fills, cards that arrive. Done in the two screens, `Fields.tsx`'s stylesheet and `portal.css`, with no dependency added and no sentence reworded: every word on both screens is the contract's and was there before, moved rather than shortened, plus one summary the Configuration card needed (`settings.json#screen.about`).

| What landed | What it refuses, and what did not move | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Speech settings, editor first.** Under the sentence saying the service reads the contract's defaults, the embedded Configuration editor, unchanged (`<Settings engine="assistant" … fixed/>`); what is in force — each register's provider, the provider cards, what is not a setting, the own-voice record, the refusals — in a second column on a screen 1200px or wider, each block a disclosure under its own heading, open there and closed on a phone, under the editor | **The screen still draws no control of its own** — a disclosure is not one, and check-boundaries' Speech settings and Phase 4 blocks pass unchanged. Every block is still a region named by its heading, closed or open. The own-voice facts, which drew "In force" one letter wide at desktop width, are a column of rows | `features/portal/gilbertone/SpeechSettings.tsx`, `lib/layout.ts` |
| **Configuration, change first.** The founder's gate, then the search, then the two sentences about the screen side by side where there is room — still above every setting — and a bar under "{shown} of {total} settings" drawing the share a search leaves. Each card in two halves: its name, what it decides, the value in force, its standing words and the Change button or the form; beside it (a container query) or under it, its default, limits and rules in a disclosure open on a wide screen and closed on a phone, and its history | **The gate stays above the editor**, and the preview sentence stays above every Change button. **The standing words never fold**: "Last changed by" or "Not changed from the default", and the review pill, stay beside the value, because the card's tint says the same and a tint with its words folded away is colour speaking alone. Embedded on Speech settings the card's rules start folded, because that column is never wide enough for two halves | `features/Configuration.tsx`, `packages/catalog/settings.json`, `surface/clinical-screens.css` (the legacy shell's wrappers) |
| **Controls that move.** A two-choice radio set is a segmented toggle — a recessed track and a dark thumb that slides to the chosen half, found by shape so the voice preview's two labels are the same toggle; the switch's knob slides, stretches under a press and shows a drawn tick; a chosen chip's disc pops and its tick draws; a slider's thumb grows and its chip lifts while held; the primary lifts on a soft green glow with a band of light crossing it once, the secondary turns to glass; the change form shows its two steps as segments that fill; a refusal is nudged once; a confirmed value lands in place of the old; cards, rows and blocks arrive in three steps and no more | **No new text pair**: every word stays on a ground it was measured on; the two translucent hover grounds are argued in the sheet with their arithmetic. **Never a glow on a focused control** (`:not(:focus-visible)`), so core.css's two rings are never overwritten. **All motion finite**, token durations and `--ease-soft` only, on transform, opacity or background-size, started only under `[data-decor='on']`, and removed with `!important` under reduced motion — including anything later added under the two screens | `features/portal/fields.css`, `features/portal/portal.css` |

**What did not move.** No route, lock line, refusal or setting changed; the one contract change is a word. **Tests:** the history's summary is found as `.cf-history > summary` in `tests/configuration.spec.ts` and `tests/gilbertone-admin.spec.ts`, because a card now has two disclosures, and the first journey also reads the new summary's words; the Speech settings journey asserts the editor comes before what is in force, and unfolds the providers' block before finding a card in it by role, as a phone's reader would. No accessibility assertion was touched. Measured by hand at 320px and at 200% with every disclosure open: no overflow inside `main`; nothing under 13px; nothing running with reduced motion asked for. The patient's first load: **281.16 kB gzipped at level 9 across 15 files**, and none of the fifteen carries a byte of this change.
